### SESSION 5 — RUSSELL'S INCOMPLETE SYMBOLS AND THE THEORY OF DESCRIPTIONS

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Phrases like "the present King of France" or "the golden mountain" look like names. If they were names, they would have to name something, and then a sentence containing one would be about a non-existent object — or would be meaningless. Russell's answer is that such phrases are not names at all. When you write the sentence out properly, the phrase disappears and is replaced by a claim that *there is exactly one thing of a certain kind, and it has a certain property*. The sentence then turns out to be plainly false rather than meaningless or mysterious.

**Technical definition:** An **incomplete symbol** is an expression that has **no meaning in isolation** but contributes systematically to the meaning of every sentence in which it occurs, and which **disappears on analysis**; it is given a **contextual definition** — a rule for eliminating whole sentences containing it — rather than a definition in its own right. The paradigm case is the **definite description**, a phrase of the form "the so-and-so". Russell's **theory of descriptions** ("On Denoting", *Mind*, 1905) analyses "The F is G" into the conjunction of three claims: **existence** — there is at least one F; **uniqueness** — there is at most one F; and **predication** — that thing is G. Formally: **(Ex)[Fx & (Ay)(Fy -> y = x) & Gx]**. ⚠️ The theory therefore replaces a referring expression by a **quantificational** structure, which is why the failure of reference produces falsity rather than meaninglessness.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Russell's theory of descriptions is the founding demonstration that grammar can lie: "the golden mountain" wears the costume of a name, but once the sentence is written in logical notation the apparent name has vanished entirely, and with it the pressure to find something for it to designate.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **incomplete symbol** — no meaning in isolation; defined contextually; disappears on analysis
- **denoting phrase** — Russell's own term for "a so-and-so", "all so-and-sos", "the so-and-so"
- **existence, uniqueness, predication** — the three conjuncts; write them, in notation
- **false, not meaningless** — the result, stated in the words the examiner is looking for
- **primary and secondary occurrence** — Russell's scope terminology
- **Meinong's jungle** ⚠️ — the ontological inflation the theory is designed to avoid
- **grammatical form is not logical form** — the general principle the theory establishes

**How to use them:** in any descriptions question, produce the **notation** early. The directive decoder in this topic is unusually strict: a script that explains "the golden mountain" in words alone has not done what "discuss in the context of Russell's theory of descriptions" asks.

#### Plain-language visual — the costume and the body

```text
   WHAT THE SENTENCE LOOKS LIKE                WHAT IT ACTUALLY SAYS
   +-----------------------------+             +-----------------------+
   | "The present King of France |             | THERE IS AT LEAST ONE |
   |  is bald."                  |             | thing that is         |
   |                             |             | presently King of     |
   |  SUBJECT:  the present King |   ------>   | France                |
   |            of France        |             |     AND               |
   |  PREDICATE: is bald         |             | THERE IS AT MOST ONE  |
   |                             |             | such thing            |
   |  Looks like: NAME + QUALITY |             |     AND               |
   +-----------------------------+             | THAT thing is bald    |
                                               +-----------------------+
                                                          |
                                                          v
                                          THE FIRST CONJUNCT IS FALSE
                                          -> the WHOLE CONJUNCTION is
                                             FALSE
                                          -> NOT meaningless, and NOT
                                             about a ghostly King
```

**In simple words:** the sentence is wearing a costume. It is dressed as "this thing has that property", and so we go looking for the thing. Strip the costume off and what is underneath is a general claim about *how many* things of a certain kind there are and *what* they are like. Since there are none, the claim is simply untrue — in the same ordinary way that "there is exactly one unicorn in this room, and it is grey" is untrue. Nothing spooky is required.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The phrase is **not a name** | it is an incomplete symbol given a contextual definition |
| Three conjuncts, in order | existence · uniqueness · predication |
| The verdict is **false** | meaninglessness is what the theory *avoids* |
| No object is posited | ⚠️ this is the reply to Meinong, not a side-remark |
| The year is **1905**, in *Mind* | Ramsey later called it "a paradigm of philosophy" |

> **One-line memory:** "the F" is a quantifier in disguise, not a name in trouble.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the problem, the three rejected answers, and the analysis

```text
   THE PROBLEM (Russell's own setting)
   "The present King of France is bald."
   Grammatically impeccable. Understood by every competent speaker.
   And there is no present King of France.
        |
   THREE ANSWERS RUSSELL REJECTS
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | (a) THE SENTENCE IS MEANINGLESS.                               |
   |     ❌ But we understand it, and we can argue about it.        |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | (b) IT IS ABOUT A NON-EXISTENT OBJECT THAT SUBSISTS.           |
   |     ❌ MEINONG's route. It populates the world with golden     |
   |        mountains and round squares, and the round square       |
   |        threatens the law of non-contradiction.                 |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | (c) "THE PRESENT KING OF FRANCE" DENOTES SOMETHING ELSE.       |
   |     ❌ Then what? No candidate survives inspection.            |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
   RUSSELL'S ANALYSIS - the phrase is eliminated, not interpreted
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   |  "The F is G"                                                  |
   |         =                                                      |
   |  (Ex)[ Fx  AND  (Ay)(Fy -> y = x)  AND  Gx ]                   |
   |          ^            ^                    ^                   |
   |          |            |                    |                   |
   |     EXISTENCE     UNIQUENESS          PREDICATION              |
   |   at least one    at most one         it is G                  |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
        |
   THE THREE PUZZLES THIS SOLVES
   +--------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | NEGATIVE           | "The present King of France does not      |
   | EXISTENTIALS       | exist" = "it is NOT the case that there   |
   |                    | is exactly one present King of France."   |
   |                    | TRUE, and nothing is denoted.             |
   +--------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | EXCLUDED MIDDLE    | "is bald" is FALSE; and its negation,     |
   |                    | correctly parsed with WIDE scope for the  |
   |                    | negation, is TRUE. No violation.          |
   +--------------------+-------------------------------------------+
   | SUBSTITUTIVITY     | George IV wished to know whether Scott    |
   | IN ATTITUDE        | was the author of Waverley. Substituting  |
   | CONTEXTS           | "Scott" would make the wish trivial. But  |
   |                    | "the author of Waverley" is NOT a name,   |
   |                    | so no substitution licence exists.        |
   +--------------------+-------------------------------------------+
        |
   SCOPE - PRIMARY AND SECONDARY OCCURRENCE
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | "The King of France is not bald."                              |
   |  PRIMARY (wide scope for the description):                     |
   |    there is exactly one King of France AND he is not bald      |
   |    -> FALSE                                                    |
   |  SECONDARY (narrow scope; negation takes wide scope):          |
   |    it is not the case that (there is exactly one King of        |
   |    France and he is bald)                                      |
   |    -> TRUE                                                     |
   | ONE sentence, TWO logical forms. Ambiguity of SCOPE, not of    |
   | meaning of the words.                                          |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The three puzzles are Russell's own advertisement for the theory: a theory that solves three independent problems with one device is not an *ad hoc* repair. Say that explicitly; it is the "why it matters" mark.

#### 1. What makes a symbol "incomplete" — the definition and its test ✅

- ✅ **Definition.** A symbol is incomplete when it has no meaning in isolation and is defined only through rules that give the meaning of **whole sentences** in which it occurs. Russell takes the technique from the practice of *contextual definition* in *Principia Mathematica*.
- ✅ **The test to apply in the exam.** Ask: *does the expression survive analysis as a constituent of the proposition?* If the correct analysis contains no corresponding constituent, the expression was an incomplete symbol. "The author of *Waverley*" fails to survive; "Scott" — as a genuine name — would survive.
- ❌ **The commonest error.** "Incomplete symbol" does **not** mean "meaningless". The phrase is systematically meaning-*contributing*; it simply has no meaning **on its own**. Write the words "in isolation" and the error is avoided.
- ⚠️ **Consequence for ontology.** Because the phrase contributes no constituent, the truth of a sentence containing it commits us to nothing beyond the existence of things satisfying the predicates. This is what makes the theory an instrument of **ontological economy** and connects it to Sessions 7 and 8.

#### 2. "The golden mountain is very high" — the 2025 question, worked ✅

1. The apparent name is "the golden mountain"; treat it as an incomplete symbol.
2. Analyse: there is at least one thing that is a golden mountain; at most one such thing; and it is very high. **(Ex)[Gx & (Ay)(Gy -> y = x) & Hx]**.
3. The **existence** conjunct is false, so the conjunction is false.
4. Therefore the sentence is **false**, not meaningless, and asserting it commits us to no golden mountain, subsisting or otherwise.
5. ⚠️ Add the scope point to secure the top band: the *negation* "the golden mountain is not very high" is **false** on the primary reading and **true** on the secondary reading, so the apparent violation of excluded middle is a scope ambiguity.

⚠️ **Why the example was chosen by the examiner.** The golden mountain is Meinong's own stock example. Naming Meinong, and saying in one clause what his position was, shows that you know what the theory was *for*.

#### 3. The three puzzles, and what each was blocking ✅

| Puzzle | What went wrong before Russell | What the analysis delivers |
|---|---|---|
| **Negative existentials** | To deny that something exists you seem to have to refer to it first, which grants it exactly the existence you are denying | The denial becomes the negation of an existence-and-uniqueness claim; no reference is attempted, so nothing is granted |
| **Excluded middle** | If "the F is G" is neither true nor false when there is no F, bivalence appears to fail | The sentence is false; its negation, parsed with the negation taking wide scope, is true; bivalence is preserved and the appearance of failure is a **scope ambiguity** |
| **Substitutivity in attitude contexts** | If "the author of *Waverley*" = Scott, George IV's curiosity becomes curiosity about whether Scott is Scott | The description is not a name and denotes nothing in the required sense, so the identity does not license substitution inside the attitude context |

#### 4. The standing objection — Strawson, and how far it reaches ❓

✅ **The objection.** P. F. Strawson, "On Referring" (*Mind*, **1950**), argues that Russell has confused a sentence with a **use** of a sentence. Someone who says "The present King of France is bald" is not *asserting* that there is exactly one King of France; he is **presupposing** it. When the presupposition fails, the question of truth or falsity **does not arise** — the utterance is not false but void.

⚠️ **Russell's available replies.**
1. Strawson's account describes conversational expectation; Russell's account gives **truth-conditions**, and a theory of truth-conditions is what a logic requires.
2. Presupposition failure needs a semantics of its own — typically a truth-value gap — and gaps carry their own costs for classical logic.
3. The analysis is not offered as ordinary-language lexicography; it is a proposal about **logical form** for use in a scientifically regimented language.

❓ **The honest verdict.** This is a genuine rival analysis rather than a demonstration of error. Most philosophers now accept that Strawson describes ordinary usage better and that Russell's quantificational treatment remains the more tractable account of logical form. ⚠️ **Owner boundary:** Strawson appears here as **one objection**; his positive theory of reference, and his descriptive metaphysics, belong to Topic 11.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** The theory of descriptions works by removing a constituent rather than by interpreting one: "the F" is shown to be an incomplete symbol that disappears under analysis, and with it the demand for something for it to designate.
> **Named evidence.** "On Denoting" (*Mind*, 1905) analyses "The F is G" as (Ex)[Fx & (Ay)(Fy -> y = x) & Gx], so that "The present King of France is bald" asserts existence, uniqueness and predication together, and is false because the first conjunct is false; the same device resolves negative existentials, apparent failures of excluded middle, and the failure of substitutivity in George IV's curiosity about the author of *Waverley*.
> **Analysis.** Because the analysis is quantificational, reference-failure produces ordinary falsity rather than meaninglessness or a Meinongian object, and the apparent violation of excluded middle is exposed as an ambiguity of **scope** between primary and secondary occurrence rather than a defect in logic.
> **Qualification.** Strawson's "On Referring" (1950) argues that existence is presupposed rather than asserted, so that the question of truth does not arise when the presupposition fails; this is a rival analysis rather than a refutation, and the durable verdict is that Russell's account of logical form has outlasted his account of ordinary use.

#### Exam link

- **2025 Q1(d)**, ten marks, is §2 exactly: notation, the false existence-conjunct, the "false not meaningless" verdict, Meinong named, and one scope sentence.
- **2023 Q4(b)**, fifteen marks, asks *why* the King-of-France proposition is "problematic": run §0's three rejected answers, then the analysis, then Strawson from §4 for the "critically".
- **2024 Q3(a)** and **2018 Q4(c)** both use this session as their first half; the second half is Sessions 7 and 8.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "The theory shows the sentence is meaningless" | It shows it is **false**. Meaninglessness is what the analysis avoids |
| "Incomplete symbol" = meaningless expression | No meaning **in isolation**; it contributes systematically in context |
| Omitting the notation | The directive in every descriptions PYQ demands the analysis, not a paraphrase |
| Omitting uniqueness | Two conjuncts are not the theory. "The" carries uniqueness; "a" does not |
| Presenting Strawson as a refutation | It is a rival analysis about use and presupposition; Russell's logic survives it |
| Treating scope as a technicality | The excluded-middle puzzle is *solved* by scope; it is a marks-bearing point |

#### Revision notes

- Incomplete symbol: no meaning in isolation · contextual definition · disappears on analysis. ❌ Not "meaningless".
- "On Denoting", *Mind*, 1905. Denoting phrases: "a so-and-so", "all so-and-sos", "the so-and-so".
- "The F is G" = (Ex)[Fx & (Ay)(Fy -> y = x) & Gx] — existence, uniqueness, predication.
- Empty description → false, not meaningless; no Meinongian subsisting object.
- Three puzzles: negative existentials · excluded middle (solved by scope) · substitutivity in attitude contexts (George IV and *Waverley*).
- Primary occurrence = description takes wide scope, sentence false; secondary occurrence = negation takes wide scope, sentence true.
- Strawson 1950: presupposition, not assertion; truth-value question does not arise. ❓ Rival analysis, not refutation; Topic 11 owns his positive theory.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — INCOMPLETE SYMBOLS AND DESCRIPTIONS

```text
PROBLEM: "The present King of France is bald" - meaningful, grammatical,
   and there is no such King
        |
        +--> REJECT: meaningless (we understand it)
        +--> REJECT: about a subsisting object (Meinong's jungle)
        +--> REJECT: denotes something else (no candidate)
        |
        v
INCOMPLETE SYMBOL: no meaning IN ISOLATION | contextual definition |
   DISAPPEARS on analysis
        |
        v
ANALYSIS: (Ex)[ Fx & (Ay)(Fy -> y=x) & Gx ]
   EXISTENCE + UNIQUENESS + PREDICATION
        |
        v
EMPTY DESCRIPTION -> first conjunct false -> WHOLE SENTENCE FALSE
   (not meaningless, no ghostly object)
        |
        v
THREE PUZZLES SOLVED
   negative existentials | excluded middle (SCOPE) | substitutivity
   (George IV / the author of Waverley)
        |
        v
SCOPE: PRIMARY (description wide) FALSE | SECONDARY (negation wide) TRUE
        |
        v
OBJECTION: STRAWSON 1950 - presupposition, not assertion; truth question
   does not arise. RIVAL ANALYSIS, not refutation. Topic 11 owns his system
ANSWER LINE: "the F" is a quantifier in disguise, not a name in trouble
```

### SESSION 6 — ACQUAINTANCE AND DESCRIPTION: THE EPISTEMOLOGY THE LOGIC WAS BUILT TO SERVE

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** Russell divides knowledge of *things* into two kinds. Some things I meet directly — this patch of red, this feeling of hardness, my own present thought. Others I have never met and never could meet — Bismarck, the electron, the centre of mass of the solar system — and about these I know only that there is exactly one thing answering to a certain description. The theory of descriptions exists to explain how the second kind of knowledge is possible without breaking the rule that I can only understand what I am acquainted with.

**Technical definition:** **Knowledge by acquaintance** is direct, presentational and **non-inferential** awareness of an item, without the intermediary of any process of inference or knowledge of truths; its objects are **sense-data**, **memory** data, **introspective** data of one's own mental states, and **universals**. **Knowledge by description** is indirect and propositional: knowledge *that* there is exactly one thing answering to a description, together with whatever is inferred about it. The controlling epistemic rule is the **Principle of Acquaintance**: ✅ *"Every proposition which we can understand must be composed wholly of constituents with which we are acquainted"* (*The Problems of Philosophy*, 1912, Ch. 5). ⚠️ In Russell's austere phase only demonstratives such as "this" and "that", used for something presently given, qualify as **logically proper names**; ordinary proper names are analysed as **disguised descriptions**.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> The theory of descriptions is not a puzzle-solving device that happens to be useful in epistemology; it is the instrument Russell built to discharge an epistemological debt, because without it his own Principle of Acquaintance would make it impossible to understand a single proposition about Bismarck, an electron, or anything else one has never met.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **acquaintance** — direct, presentational, non-inferential; "without the intermediary of any process of inference"
- **knowledge by description** — the *there is exactly one F* structure
- **the Principle of Acquaintance** — quote it verbatim; it is safe from *Problems*, Ch. 5
- **sense-data** — the objects of acquaintance in this phase, along with universals
- **logically proper name** — "this", "that", used of what is presently given
- **disguised description** ⚠️ — the account of ordinary names that Kripke later attacks
- **the Bismarck series** — Russell's own graded example; use it, it is memorable and it is his

**How to use them:** any answer on descriptions that adds this session moves from competent to first-class, because it says *why the machinery was built*. State the Principle, state the problem it creates, and present descriptions as the solution.

#### Plain-language visual — the two routes to a thing

```text
                         HOW CAN I THINK ABOUT X?
                                    |
              +---------------------+---------------------+
              |                                           |
      ROUTE 1: ACQUAINTANCE                      ROUTE 2: DESCRIPTION
      I am directly presented with X.            I have never met X.
      No inference. No middleman.                I know only: THERE IS
                                                 EXACTLY ONE thing that
      WHAT QUALIFIES:                            is F - and it is G.
        sense-data (this red patch)
        memory data                              WHAT QUALIFIES:
        introspective data (my own                 physical objects
          present awareness)                       other minds
        universals (redness, before,               historical persons
          diversity)                               unobservables of science
              |                                           |
             v                                          v
      THE BASE. Everything else                  BUILT OUT OF the base by
      is built from here.                        logical construction and
                                                 quantification.
              |                                           |
              +---------------------+---------------------+
                                    |
                                    v
                    THE RULE THAT FORCES THE STRUCTURE
      "Every proposition which we can understand must be composed wholly
       of constituents with which we are acquainted."
```

**In simple words:** you cannot think *with* something you have never encountered. So how do you manage to think about Bismarck? Not by having Bismarck in your thought, but by having in your thought only concepts you *have* encountered — being a chancellor, being German, being first — arranged into the claim that exactly one thing satisfies them. Bismarck himself never enters your mind; a description does his work.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Two kinds of **knowledge of things** | acquaintance is direct; description is propositional and indirect |
| Universals are objects of acquaintance | ⚠️ a large concession for an empiricist — say so |
| The Principle is a **constraint on understanding** | not a constraint on truth |
| Descriptions **rescue** the Principle | the constituents become universals and logical apparatus |
| Ordinary names are **disguised descriptions** | ⚠️ phase-specific, and the part Kripke destroys |

> **One-line memory:** I cannot think with what I have never met — so I think with a description instead.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the argument in six steps, and the two objections it must survive

```text
   THE ARGUMENT, NUMBERED
   1  To understand a proposition is to grasp its constituents.
   2  I can grasp only what I am acquainted with.
   3  Yet I plainly understand propositions about Bismarck, Julius
      Caesar and electrons - with none of which I am acquainted.
   4  So EITHER the Principle is false, OR such propositions do not
      really contain those items as constituents.
   5  THE THEORY OF DESCRIPTIONS takes the second route: "the F is G"
      contains NO constituent "the F" at all. It unpacks into an
      existentially quantified sentence whose constituents are the
      UNIVERSALS F and G plus logical apparatus - all acquaintance
      items.
   6  Therefore the Principle is preserved.
   =====================================================================
   THE BISMARCK SERIES - Russell's own graded example
   +---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   | BISMARCK HIMSELF          | acquaintance with his own self-     |
   |                           | consciousness; the constituent is   |
   |                           | given to him                        |
   +---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   | SOMEONE WHO MET BISMARCK  | acquaintance with sense-data he took|
   |                           | to be of the man; the description is|
   |                           | anchored to HIS particulars         |
   +---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   | SOMEONE WHO ONLY READ OF  | purely descriptive: "the first      |
   | BISMARCK                  | Chancellor of the German Empire"    |
   +---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   | ALL OF US, OF "THE MOST   | a description satisfied by someone  |
   | LONG-LIVED OF MEN"        | we may never identify - and the     |
   |                           | proposition is still FULLY          |
   |                           | UNDERSTOOD                          |
   +---------------------------+-------------------------------------+
   Russell's moral: we have MERELY DESCRIPTIVE knowledge of the so-and-so
   when we do not know any proposition "a is the so-and-so" where a is
   something with which we are acquainted.
   =====================================================================
   THE TWO OBJECTIONS THAT SHAPE THE MODERN VERDICT
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | KRIPKE, Naming and Necessity (lectures 1970, book 1980)        |
   | Ordinary proper names are NOT disguised descriptions.          |
   | "Aristotle" would still name Aristotle even if every           |
   | description we associate with him turned out false. Names are  |
   | RIGID DESIGNATORS.                                             |
   | RUSSELL'S RETREAT: confine the claim to LOGICALLY proper names |
   | and abandon the description theory of ordinary names.          |
   | ⚠️ STANDARD VERDICT: the LOGIC of descriptions survives;       |
   |    the THEORY OF NAMES does not.                               |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
   | SELLARS, "Empiricism and the Philosophy of Mind" (1956)        |
   | The MYTH OF THE GIVEN: nothing is given uninterpreted, and     |
   | what is given non-conceptually cannot justify beliefs.         |
   | RUSSELL'S REPLY: acquaintance is presentation, not knowledge   |
   | of truths.                                                     |
   | ❓ RESIDUAL: then how does it GROUND anything? This is the     |
   |    live weakness of the whole edifice.                         |
   +---------------------------------------------------------------+
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The six numbered steps are the answer to "why does Russell need the theory of descriptions?" The two boxes are the assessment. Note that they attack different halves — Kripke the theory of *names*, Sellars the theory of *acquaintance* — and that Russell can survive one without surviving the other.

#### 1. What acquaintance is, exactly ✅

- ✅ **Direct and non-inferential.** The object of acquaintance is one of which we are "directly aware… without the intermediary of any process of inference or any knowledge of truths". Acquaintance is a **relation to a thing**, not the possession of a proposition.
- ✅ **The inventory in this phase.** Sense-data; memory data; introspective data of one's own mental states; and **universals** — redness, diversity, the relation *before*.
- ⚠️ **The concession that must be flagged.** Admitting acquaintance with universals is a substantial **Platonic** commitment for a philosopher in the British empiricist line, and Russell accepts it openly against Berkeley's nominalism. Marking this is the sort of one-clause discrimination that separates scripts.
- ⚠️ **The phase warning.** Sense-data acquaintance, the 1918 atomism and the later **neutral monism** are three different positions held at three different times. ❌ Do not present them as one timeless Russellian doctrine.

#### 2. Logically proper names, and why the class is so small ✅

✅ A **logically proper name** is a symbol that directly designates an item of acquaintance and could not fail to designate. In Russell's austere phase the only serious candidates are the demonstratives **"this"** and **"that"**, used of something presently given in sense-experience.

⚠️ Two consequences follow, and both are examinable.
1. Ordinary names — "Bismarck", "Socrates", "Homer" — are **disguised definite descriptions**, and sentences containing them are analysed by the theory of descriptions.
2. The set of genuine names therefore shrinks almost to nothing, and it shrinks *differently for each speaker and each moment*, since what is presently given differs. Russell was fully aware of how restrictive this is.

❓ **Where it breaks.** Kripke's modal argument is decisive against the account of ordinary names: had the descriptions we associate with "Aristotle" been satisfied by someone else, the name would still have picked out Aristotle. ⚠️ Russell can retreat to the narrower claim about logically proper names, but the retreat is a real loss.

#### 3. How this session changes a descriptions answer ⚠️

| Answer without this session | Answer with it |
|---|---|
| Descriptions solve three puzzles about denoting | Descriptions **discharge an epistemological debt**: they let Russell keep the Principle of Acquaintance while speaking of what he has never met |
| The theory is a piece of logical grammar | The theory is the **junction** at which Russell's logic and his epistemology meet |
| Strawson is the objection | Strawson attacks the semantics; **Kripke** attacks the theory of names and **Sellars** attacks the base — three different objections at three different places |

⚠️ **The executable verdict.** "Acquaintance and description is where Russell's logic and his epistemology meet: the theory of descriptions is the price he pays to keep the Principle of Acquaintance while speaking of what he has never met. The logical machinery has proved permanent; the epistemology it was built to serve — sense-data as bedrock, ordinary names as disguised descriptions — has not survived Sellars and Kripke. That asymmetry is the most instructive fact about Russell's philosophy."

#### 4. The bridge forward ✅

Acquaintance also sets up the next two sessions. If the only items I meet directly are sense-data and universals, then **physical objects** are not met directly — and the question of how I am entitled to talk about them becomes urgent. Russell's answer is the doctrine of **logical constructions** (Session 7), and the residue that analysis is supposed to leave behind is what **logical atomism** calls an atom (Session 8). The three doctrines are one programme run in three registers: epistemic, methodological and ontological.

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Russell's distinction between knowledge by acquaintance and knowledge by description is not an appendix to the theory of descriptions but its motive: the logic exists to make the epistemology survivable.
> **Named evidence.** *The Problems of Philosophy* (1912, Ch. 5), following the 1910–11 Aristotelian Society paper, states the Principle of Acquaintance — "Every proposition which we can understand must be composed wholly of constituents with which we are acquainted" — and grades the Bismarck case from Bismarck's own self-consciousness, through the acquaintance of someone who met him, to the purely descriptive knowledge of a reader, and finally to "the most long-lived of men", a description whose satisfier we may never identify.
> **Analysis.** Since I plainly understand propositions about Bismarck without being acquainted with him, either the Principle fails or such propositions do not contain him as a constituent; the theory of descriptions takes the second route, replacing the apparent constituent with universals and logical apparatus that *are* items of acquaintance.
> **Qualification.** The rescue is bought at two prices that later philosophy has refused to pay — Kripke's modal argument shows that ordinary names are rigid designators rather than disguised descriptions, and Sellars's attack on the Myth of the Given asks how a non-propositional presentation could justify anything — so the durable position is that Russell's logical instrument outlived the epistemology it was designed to protect.

#### Exam link

- **2024 Q3(a)** and **2018 Q4(c)** are strengthened by one paragraph from this session, placed immediately after the definition of the incomplete symbol: it explains *why* Russell wanted the elimination in the first place.
- **2023 Q4(b)** and **2025 Q1(d)** can each use one sentence — the Principle of Acquaintance — as the reason the analysis is not arbitrary.
- **2018 Q3(a)** (Empiricism-owned): Russell's reaction to Berkeley is built here and in Session 7 — sense-data accepted, physical objects reconstructed.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| Treating acquaintance as a kind of *propositional* knowledge | It is a relation to a **thing**, non-inferential and not knowledge of truths |
| Forgetting universals | They are objects of acquaintance, and the admission is a real break with the empiricist line |
| Presenting one timeless Russell | Sense-data acquaintance, 1918 atomism and later neutral monism are three phases |
| Making Kripke and Sellars the same objection | Kripke attacks the theory of **names**; Sellars attacks the **given**. Different targets, different residues |
| Saying Kripke refutes the theory of descriptions | He refutes its extension to **ordinary proper names**. The analysis of definite descriptions stands |

#### Revision notes

- Acquaintance: direct, presentational, non-inferential; objects are sense-data, memory data, introspective data and universals.
- Description: knowledge that exactly one thing is F, plus what is inferred of it; covers physical objects, other minds, history, unobservables.
- Principle of Acquaintance (*Problems*, 1912, Ch. 5), quotable verbatim.
- Six-step argument: understanding is constituent-grasping → I grasp only what I meet → yet I understand Bismarck-propositions → so either the Principle fails or the constituent is absent → descriptions remove it → Principle preserved.
- Bismarck series: himself · one who met him · one who read of him · "the most long-lived of men".
- Logically proper names: "this", "that", of what is presently given; ordinary names are disguised descriptions ⚠️.
- Objections: Kripke 1970/1980 on rigid designation (names) · Sellars 1956 on the Myth of the Given (base). Verdict: the logic survives, the epistemology does not.

#### CLOSING RECALL FLOW — ACQUAINTANCE AND DESCRIPTION

```text
PRINCIPLE OF ACQUAINTANCE (Problems, 1912, Ch. 5)
   "Every proposition which we can understand must be composed wholly of
    constituents with which we are acquainted."
        |
        v
ACQUAINTANCE = direct, non-inferential presentation
   sense-data | memory | introspection | UNIVERSALS (the Platonic
   concession)
        |
        v
PROBLEM: I understand propositions about BISMARCK, CAESAR, ELECTRONS -
   none of which I have met
        |

DILEMMA: either the Principle is false, or those items are NOT
   constituents of the propositions
        |
        v
DESCRIPTIONS TAKE THE SECOND ROUTE -> "the F is G" has NO constituent
   "the F"; its constituents are UNIVERSALS + logical apparatus
        |
        v
BISMARCK SERIES: himself | one who met him | one who read of him |
   "the most long-lived of men"
        |
        v

   DISGUISED DESCRIPTIONS ⚠️
        |
        v
OBJECTIONS: KRIPKE (rigid designators, names) | SELLARS (Myth of the
   Given, base)
VERDICT: the logical machinery is permanent; the epistemology it served
   is not
ANSWER LINE: the theory of descriptions is the price Russell pays to keep
   the Principle of Acquaintance
```

### SESSION 7 — LOGICAL CONSTRUCTIONS AND THE SUPREME MAXIM

#### DEFINITION / WHAT THIS IS CALLED

**Plain-language definition:** If I have never met a physical object directly — only patches of colour, shapes and feels — then saying that a table exists *behind* my experiences is an inference to something unknown. Russell proposes a different move. Instead of inferring a hidden table, define "the table" in terms of an orderly system of actual and possible appearances that I *do* meet. The table then stops being a guess and becomes a construction out of things already known.

**Technical definition:** A **logical construction** is an entity-talk that is given a **contextual definition** in terms of items already available, so that sentences about the apparent entity are translated into sentences about the base items and no *additional* entity is posited. The programme is governed by Russell's **supreme maxim in scientific philosophising** ("The Relation of Sense-Data to Physics" and *Our Knowledge of the External World*, both **1914**): ✅ *"Wherever possible, substitute constructions out of known entities for inferences to unknown entities."* ⚠️ The doctrine is a **period-specific programme** and not Russell's permanent final ontology; the later **neutral monism**, which reconceives mental and physical items in terms of neutral events, is a different position and is not a printed limb of this clause.

#### ANSWER-GRABBING OPENING — WRITE/ADAPT IN THE EXAM

> Russell's doctrine of logical constructions is Ockham's razor turned into a working procedure: wherever a philosopher is tempted to *infer* an entity he cannot meet in order to explain what he can, Russell asks him first to try *building* the explanation from what he already has — and the table, the nation, the number and the point of space all turn out to be buildable.

#### MUST-WRITE KEYWORDS

- **the supreme maxim in scientific philosophising** — quote it; it is the whole doctrine in one sentence
- **constructions out of known entities against inferences to unknown entities** — the contrast that generates the maxim
- **contextual definition** — the technical link back to incomplete symbols
- **ontological economy** — the purpose, in the examiner's vocabulary
- **actual and possible sense-data** — the base for the physical-object construction in this phase
- ⚠️ **not fictions** — constructions are not pretences; true things are said about tables and nations
- ⚠️ **phase-specific** — do not merge the 1914 programme with later neutral monism

**How to use them:** state the maxim, then give **at least two examples of different types** — a physical object and a non-physical one such as a nation or a number — because the range is what shows the maxim is a general method rather than a solution to one problem.

#### Plain-language visual — infer, or build?

```text
   THE SITUATION
   What I actually meet:   [ patch ] [ patch ] [ patch ] [ patch ] ...
                            colours, shapes, feels, from many viewpoints

   THE QUESTION            What entitles me to talk about A TABLE?
        |
   +----+---------------------------+---------------------------+
   |                                                            |
   ROUTE A: INFERENCE                          ROUTE B: CONSTRUCTION
   +-------------------------------+   +-----------------------------+
   | Posit a table BEHIND the      |   | DEFINE "the table" as an    |
   | appearances, causing them,    |   | ordered system of ACTUAL    |
   | never itself met.             |   | and POSSIBLE appearances    |
   |                               |   | from various viewpoints.    |
   | COST: an entity I can never   |   |                             |
   | check, doing explanatory work |   | COST: the system must       |
   | I cannot inspect.             |   | really be specifiable.      |
   +-------------------------------+   +-----------------------------+
        |                                              |
       v                                             v
   RISK: unknowable posits multiply         GAIN: no new entity; the
   and scepticism gets a foothold           truth-conditions are stated
                                            in terms already understood
        |                                              |
        +---------------------> MAXIM <----------------+
        "Wherever possible, substitute constructions out of known
         entities for inferences to unknown entities."
```

**In simple words:** suppose you keep receiving letters signed "the committee" and you are wondering whether there is a committee-shaped object somewhere. Russell's advice is: do not go looking for the object. Say instead what makes a sentence about the committee true — certain people meeting, deciding, signing — and you will find that everything you wanted to say can be said without any extra thing existing. That is a logical construction.

| Remember first | Meaning |
|---|---|
| The maxim is **methodological** | it tells you what to try first, not what reality contains |
| Constructions get **contextual** definitions | the same technique as incomplete symbols |
| They are **not fictions** | "there is a nation" can be true; it just posits no extra entity |
| The base is **phase-specific** | in 1914 it is actual and possible sense-data |
| The range is wide | tables, nations, numbers, points, instants |

> **One-line memory:** build it out of what you have before you posit what you cannot meet.

#### 🖼️ VISUAL — the four standard constructions and the atom/construction contrast

```text
   FOUR WORKED EXAMPLES - learn at least two of different types
   +--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | APPARENT ENTITY    | CONSTRUCTED OUT OF                       |
   +--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | A physical object  | classes of ACTUAL and POSSIBLE sense-data |
   | ("this table")     | from various points of view              |
   +--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | A "nation"         | patterns of behaviour of individual      |
   |                    | persons                                  |
   +--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | A number           | classes of equinumerous classes          |
   |                    | (the Frege-Russell definition)           |
   +--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   | A point of space   | convergent classes of volumes            |
   +--------------------+------------------------------------------+
   NOTE THE SPREAD: one perceptual, one social, one mathematical, one
   geometrical. That spread is the evidence that the maxim is a METHOD.
   =====================================================================
   LOGICAL ATOMS  vs  LOGICAL CONSTRUCTIONS - the contrast that is tested
   +-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
   | LOGICAL ATOMS (basic)         | LOGICAL CONSTRUCTIONS (derived)|
   +-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
   | genuinely exist as independent| add NO further entity; the     |
   | simples within the analysis   | SYMBOLS for them are defined   |
   |                               | CONTEXTUALLY                   |
   | treated as basic constituents | derived discourse receives a   |
   | of atomic facts               | contextual construction        |
   | the GROUND of knowledge       | convenient shorthand for       |
   |                               | complexes of atoms             |
   +-------------------------------+-------------------------------+
   =====================================================================
   THE THREE THINGS A CONSTRUCTION IS NOT
   ❌ NOT a fiction - we say TRUE things about tables and nations
   ❌ NOT an elimination of ordinary talk - the talk is preserved and
      given truth-conditions
   ❌ NOT a claim that the object is "really" a bundle of experiences in
      some phenomenalist metaphysics that Russell held permanently -
      ⚠️ it is a proposed reduction / structural reconstruction, and
      Russell's own commitments changed
```

⚠️ **Caption.** The right-hand column of the second box is the answer to "how do constructions relate to atomism?" — constructions are what analysis *removes*, atoms are what analysis is *looking for*. The two doctrines are the two ends of one procedure.

#### 1. Why the maxim is a razor, and what kind of razor ✅

- ✅ **Occam's razor, applied to inference rather than to entities in general.** Russell's target is a specific bad habit: explaining what we meet by positing what we cannot. The maxim does not say "there are no unknown entities"; it says that a construction, where available, is **epistemically preferable** because its adequacy can be checked.
- ⚠️ **What follows and what does not.** ✅ It follows that we should not *infer* a table if a construction does the same work. ⚠️ It does not follow that there is no table, nor that "table" is a false or empty word. Russell's claim is about the **entities our theory must posit**, not about the truth of ordinary sentences.
- ✅ **The name.** Russell himself calls it the **supreme maxim in scientific philosophising**; the phrase is safe to quote and signals that he intends a general method for philosophy modelled on scientific parsimony.

#### 2. The connection to incomplete symbols — one technique, two applications ✅

| Incomplete symbols (Session 5) | Logical constructions (this session) |
|---|---|
| "The F" has no meaning in isolation and disappears on analysis | "The table" is given a contextual construction and posits no extra entity |
| Removes a **pseudo-constituent** from a proposition | Removes a **pseudo-posit** from an ontology |
| Device: quantificational paraphrase | Device: definition in terms of ordered classes of base items |
| Result: the sentence is false rather than meaningless when nothing satisfies | Result: the sentence is true, and its truth requires only base items |

- ✅ **The unifying idea.** In both cases an expression that looks as if it must designate is given rules for the **whole sentences** in which it occurs. Russell's phrase for this in *Principia Mathematica* is contextual definition; "incomplete symbol" is the name for expressions so defined.
- ⚠️ **The difference that must not be blurred.** A definite description with no satisfier makes its sentence **false**. A logical construction is not a failure case at all — sentences about tables and nations are typically **true**; what is denied is only that their truth requires an additional entity.

#### 3. The strain in the physical-object construction ❓

✅ **The problem of possible sense-data.** To construct a table Russell must appeal not only to sense-data actually occurring but to those that *would* occur from viewpoints no one occupies — "sensibilia". This is where the programme is most exposed.

| Objection ❓ | Reply available to Russell ⚠️ | Residual |
|---|---|---|
| Possible sense-data are not *known* entities, so the construction has smuggled back the inference it was meant to avoid | The appeal is to a **structural** system of appearances, specifiable in general terms, rather than to a hidden particular | ❓ Whether a system of unrealised possibilities is genuinely more economical than one physical object is still disputed |
| Building the world from private sense-data invites **solipsism** | Constructions are public-theoretical reconstructions aimed at explanatory economy, not a denial of common objects or of science | ⚠️ The reply is available; it does not by itself supply an account of how different observers' bases connect |
| The base itself is unstable, since Russell later abandoned sense-data for neutral events | ✅ Correct, and it should be conceded: the 1914 construction is a **phase** | ⚠️ Concede it and the answer gains credit for provenance discipline rather than losing it |

- ⚠️ **The exam-safe verdict.** The **technique** of construction is a permanent contribution to philosophical method and survives independently of the phenomenalist base; the specific **1914 construction of physical objects out of actual and possible sense-data** is strained and was not retained. Distinguishing the two is what "critically discuss" asks for.

#### 4. Where constructions sit in Russell's programme ✅

1. **Acquaintance** (Session 6) fixes what is directly available: sense-data, memory, introspection, universals.
2. **Incomplete symbols** (Session 5) supply the technique for eliminating apparent designators.
3. **Logical constructions** (this session) apply the technique to the entities of common sense and science, so that nothing unknown need be inferred.
4. **Logical atomism** (Session 8) names the residue: whatever analysis leaves standing when every construction has been removed.

⚠️ **The single sentence that connects them for a twenty-mark answer.** "Incomplete symbols supply the scalpel, logical constructions are the operations performed with it, and logical atomism is the anatomy the surgeon expects to find — which is why Russell can be confident in the technique long before he is confident about the inventory."

#### HOW TO WRITE THE PARAGRAPH — claim → named evidence → analysis → qualification

> **Claim.** Logical constructions convert Ockham's razor from a slogan into a procedure, replacing inference to unknown entities with definition in terms of known ones.
> **Named evidence.** Russell's supreme maxim in scientific philosophising, stated in "The Relation of Sense-Data to Physics" and *Our Knowledge of the External World* (both 1914), is "wherever possible, substitute constructions out of known entities for inferences to unknown entities"; the worked cases run from physical objects, built from classes of actual and possible sense-data, to nations built from patterns of individual behaviour, numbers as classes of equinumerous classes, and points of space as convergent classes of volumes.
> **Analysis.** The device is the contextual definition already used for incomplete symbols, transferred from pseudo-constituents of propositions to pseudo-posits of ontology, so that ordinary sentences retain their truth while the theory retains only base items; the spread of examples across perception, society, mathematics and geometry shows that the maxim is a general method rather than a repair to one problem.
> **Qualification.** The construction of physical objects requires unrealised "possible" sense-data, which reintroduces something not straightforwardly known, and Russell's own base shifted from sense-data to neutral events in his later work — so the durable claim is for the technique of construction, not for the 1914 phenomenalist inventory it was first used to build.

#### Exam link

- **2024 Q3(a)** and **2018 Q4(c)** both need this session as the middle term: incomplete symbols → constructions → atomism is the route the questions ask you to trace.
- **2022 Q1(b)** ("Russell's method of logical analysis") should give the maxim verbatim; it is the clearest single statement of the method.
- **2018 Q3(a)** (Empiricism-owned): Russell's reply to Berkeley is exactly this — accept that we are given appearances, and then *construct* rather than *deny* the physical world.

#### UPSC traps

| Trap | Correction |
|---|---|
| "Logical constructions = fictions" | We say **true** things about tables and nations; what is denied is an additional entity, not the truth of the sentences |
| Merging constructions with later neutral monism | 1914 constructions and later neutral monism are different phases; say which you are describing |
| Giving only the table example | Give a second of a different type — nation, number or point — to show the maxim is general |
| Treating constructions and atoms as the same | Constructions are removed by analysis; atoms are what analysis is looking for |
| Omitting the "possible sense-data" strain | It is the standard objection and the easiest assessment mark in the session |

#### Revision notes

- Supreme maxim (1914): "wherever possible, substitute constructions out of known entities for inferences to unknown entities".
- Sources: "The Relation of Sense-Data to Physics"; *Our Knowledge of the External World*.
- Examples: physical object ← classes of actual and possible sense-data · nation ← patterns of individual behaviour · number ← classes of equinumerous classes · point ← convergent classes of volumes.
- Device: contextual definition — the same technique as incomplete symbols, applied to ontology rather than to propositional constituents.
- Atoms vs constructions: atoms are the sought residue; constructions add no entity and are contextually defined.
- ❌ Not fictions · ❌ not an elimination of ordinary talk · ⚠️ not a permanent phenomenalist metaphysics.
- Strain: possible sense-data; solipsism worry; base later abandoned for neutral events. Verdict: technique permanent, 1914 inventory not.

```text
        |
        v
TWO ROUTES: INFER an unknown entity  |  BUILD from known entities
        |
        v
SUPREME MAXIM IN SCIENTIFIC PHILOSOPHISING (1914)
   "Wherever possible, substitute constructions out of known entities
    for inferences to unknown entities."
        |
        v
DEVICE: CONTEXTUAL DEFINITION - the incomplete-symbol technique applied
   to ONTOLOGY instead of to propositional constituents
        |
        v
WORKED CASES: table <- classes of actual + possible sense-data |
   nation <- patterns of individual behaviour | number <- classes of
   equinumerous classes | point <- convergent classes of volumes
        |
        v
CONSTRUCTIONS (derived, no new entity)  vs  ATOMS (sought residue)
        |
        v
STRAIN: "possible" sense-data are not straightforwardly KNOWN;
   solipsism worry; the base later becomes NEUTRAL EVENTS

   is a phase
ANSWER LINE: build it out of what you have before you posit what you
   cannot meet
```
