### A4. Russell's paradox and the theory of types ⚠️ — background, not a printed limb

- **What it is.** The class of all classes that are not members of themselves both is and is not a member of itself. Russell's response is a **theory of types**: expressions are stratified so that a class cannot significantly be said to belong to itself, and a sentence violating the stratification is not false but **ill-formed**.
- **Why it appears here at all.** It supplies the intellectual habit behind the doctrine of incomplete symbols: a symbol may be **eliminable** rather than referential, and a well-formed-looking string may fail to express a proposition. The technique of **contextual definition** in *Principia Mathematica* is the same technique the theory of descriptions applies to "the F".
- ⚠️ **Owner boundary.** The owner file classes Russell's paradox, the theory of types, logicism and later neutral monism as **optional orientation unless a question explicitly asks for them**. They are **not** printed limbs of item 6. One sentence of background is the maximum a Topic 06 answer should spend.
- ❌ **Never** substitute a discussion of types for the analysis of descriptions in a question about incomplete symbols.

### A5. Solipsism and the metaphysical subject at the limit ⚠️

- **What the text says.** "The limits of my language mean the limits of my world" (5.6). "What the solipsist *means* is quite correct, only it cannot be *said*: it shows itself" (5.62). The subject "does not belong to the world; rather, it is a limit of the world" (5.632), and the eye is not in the visual field (5.633). Strictly maintained, "solipsism coincides with pure realism" (5.64).
- **Why it is here.** It is the most vivid illustration of the say/show boundary: an apparently metaphysical thesis is neither affirmed nor denied but relocated to the side of what shows itself.
- ⚠️ **Owner boundary.** The owner file treats solipsism and the metaphysical subject as **optional** in this clause. Use them to illustrate the boundary; do not build a section on them. ❌ The critique of the private language argument belongs to Topic 08 and must not be retrojected into the *Tractatus*.

### A6. Moore's naturalistic fallacy — named here only to be excluded ❌

- **What it is.** In *Principia Ethica* (1903) Moore argues that goodness is a simple, unanalysable, non-natural property, and that any definition of "good" in natural terms commits the **naturalistic fallacy**; the **Open Question Argument** is the test — for any proposed natural definition, "but is that good?" remains an open question.
- ❌ **Why it is not owned here.** It belongs to **ethics**, and the owner file states explicitly that it is not printed in this clause. Same author, same year as "The Refutation of Idealism", entirely different subject.
- ⚠️ **The only legitimate use.** A single clause of scope-setting in an answer on Moore, distinguishing the epistemological Moore of this syllabus item from the ethical Moore of *Principia Ethica*. Anything more is answering a question the paper did not ask.

### Conceptual links — not current-affairs items

⚠️ This clause has **no live current-affairs linkage**, and manufacturing one would be a fault rather than a merit. Its live connections are conceptual and internal to the syllabus: forward to **Logical Positivism** (Topic 07), which transforms Tractarian resources into an empiricist criterion of significance that the *Tractatus* does not contain; forward to **Later Wittgenstein** (Topic 08), which criticises the early programme from inside; sideways to **Quine and Strawson** (Topic 11), where Strawson's presuppositional account of reference and Quine's holism press on the analytic assumptions of the whole movement; and backwards to **Empiricism** (Topic 03) and **Hegel** (Topic 05), which supply the two idealisms the revolt distinguishes between.

### Source trail for this block

Advanced dossier §6, optional depth modules 1–3 (descriptions after the textbook solution; logical constructions against phenomenal reduction; what "showing" means), with the short source trail naming Stephen Neale, Peter Hylton, David Pears, P. M. S. Hacker, Cora Diamond and James Conant; the owner file's "Cross-owned or optional" list for Russell's paradox, the theory of types, logicism and neutral monism, for Moore's naturalistic fallacy and Open Question Argument, and for the bounded treatment of solipsism and the metaphysical subject.

## CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

> Compressed revision notes for the complete topic. They cover every subtopic in the Basic block and preserve the Basic / Optional-Advanced distinction; they do not repeat the teaching introductions. Read them after the sessions, not instead of them. The complete ASCII master flow diagram closes the package.

### A. Attribution map — who owns what, and what is not owned at all

- **Seven printed terms, three owners.** Defence of Commonsense — **Moore**, "A Defence of Common Sense" (1925). Refutation of Idealism — **Moore**, *Mind* (1903). Logical Atomism — **Russell** (1918 lectures) **and distinctly Wittgenstein** (*Tractatus*). Logical Constructions — **Russell** (1914). Incomplete Symbols — **Russell**, "On Denoting" (1905) and *Principia Mathematica*. Picture Theory of Meaning — **early Wittgenstein**. Saying and Showing — **early Wittgenstein**, 4.12–4.1212, 6.522, 6.54, 7. The paper prints "Sying and Showing"; retain that wording only when quoting the syllabus.
- 🔑 **Mnemonic:** Moore **DEFENDS**, Russell **DISSECTS**, Wittgenstein **PICTURES**.
- **Three senses of "analysis":** commitment-clarification (Moore) · quantificational paraphrase (Russell) · resolution into truth-functions of elementary propositions (early Wittgenstein). The word is shared; the method is not.
- ❌ **Not owned:** the verification principle and the rejection of metaphysics as doctrine (Topic 07) · meaning as use, language-games, family resemblance, private language (Topic 08) · Strawson's positive semantics and theory of persons (Topic 11) · Berkeley's immaterialism as doctrine (Topic 03) · Hegel's Absolute Idealism as doctrine (Topic 05) · **Moore's naturalistic fallacy and Open Question Argument** (ethics) · Russell's logicism, paradox, theory of types and later neutral monism as systems.
- **The target:** **British Absolute Idealism** — internal relations, degrees of truth, the ordinary world as "appearance" (Bradley, McTaggart). ⚠️ Moore's 1903 paper attacks the **Berkeleyan** *esse est percipi* equation, not Absolute Idealism; say which idealism you mean.

### B. Chronology and provenance — examiner-checked

- **1893** Bradley, *Appearance and Reality* — the target in place. **1903** Moore, "The Refutation of Idealism" (*Mind*) **and** *Principia Ethica* — same year, different subjects. **1905** Russell, "On Denoting" (*Mind*). **1910–11** "Knowledge by Acquaintance and Knowledge by Description" (*PAS*). **1910–13** *Principia Mathematica* — contextual definition. **1912** *The Problems of Philosophy*, Ch. 5 — the Principle of Acquaintance. **1914** *Our Knowledge of the External World* and "The Relation of Sense-Data to Physics" — the supreme maxim. **1918** Russell's lectures (published *The Monist* 1918–19) **and** the completed *Tractatus* manuscript. **1921** German *Logisch-Philosophische Abhandlung*. **1922** bilingual *Tractatus*, Latin title suggested by **Moore**, Ogden translation with Ramsey's assistance, checked by Wittgenstein. **1925** "A Defence of Common Sense". **1929** "Some Remarks on Logical Form" — colour exclusion. **1939** "Proof of an External World". **1950** Strawson, "On Referring". **1961** Pears–McGuinness translation. **1970/1980** Kripke, *Naming and Necessity*.
- ⚠️ **Three separate Moore papers, three separate arguments** — merging 1925 and 1939 is the commonest error in scripts.
- ⚠️ **Never** date the English *Tractatus* 1921. ⚠️ **Never** say Russell invented logical atomism unaided — his own 1918 preface credits Wittgenstein.
- ⚠️ **Decimal citation is the safest citation in the Western syllabus:** the numbering is identical in every edition and translation. **There is no proposition 7.1.**
- ⚠️ **Translation flags:** *Sachverhalt* = "atomic fact" (Ogden) / "state of affairs" (Pears–McGuinness). Proposition 7 = "Whereof one cannot speak, thereof one must be silent" (Ogden) / "What we cannot speak about we must pass over in silence" (Pears–McGuinness). 4.5 = "Such and such is the case" (Ogden) / "This is how things stand" (Pears–McGuinness). "Picture theory" is the commentators' label; Wittgenstein writes *Bild* and *abbilden*.

### C. Moore — the defence of common sense

- **1925: the list of truisms** — a living human body which is his body; its continuous existence on or near the earth; the earth's existence for many years past; other human beings with bodies — asserted as **known**, plus the second-order claim that many others know propositions of the same kind.
- **Knowing that P is not knowing the analysis of P.** Analytic difficulty about "material object" cannot be converted into ignorance that one has hands: that is a **confusion of levels**.
- **Comparative certainty:** an argument overturns a truism only if **every** premiss is more certain than the truism denied; none has ever been. **Result:** the burden of proof is reversed.
- ❌ Not popularity, not convention, not infallibility. The list is a philosophical proposal and is contestable.
- ⚠️ **Paradox of analysis** (bounded orientation): an analysis is either trivially uninformative or not an analysis of the same concept. No settled Moorean solution; Fregean sense and later accounts of concepts are proposed responses. Use it only to show why analysis is not verbal substitution.
- ⚠️ **Limit:** no **neutral measure** of comparative certainty is supplied.

### D. Moore — the proof of an external world

- **1939 British Academy lecture**, answering Kant's "scandal to philosophy" charge. **"External" = things met with in space** — the gloss that makes the inference valid.
- **The argument:** here is one hand → and here is another → at least two things met with in space exist → an external world exists. ⚠️ The printed order is **right hand first, then left**.
- **Three conditions of a rigorous proof:** premiss **different** from the conclusion · premiss **known**, not merely believed · conclusion **follows**.
- **The concession:** he cannot **prove** the premiss, because he cannot prove he is not dreaming — and a proof requires a *known* premiss, not a *proved* one, or nothing is ever proved.
- **Objection:** begs the question. **Replies:** comparative certainty (Moore's own) · dialectical burden-shift ⚠️ · framework or hinge certainty ⚠️ ❓ — the last from *On Certainty*, **Topic 08**, named in a clause and no more.
- **Verdict:** valid proof, no neutral criterion of knowledge, comparative force — exactly what was claimed.

### E. Moore — the refutation of idealism

- **1903, *Mind*.** Target: **to be is to be perceived (*esse est percipi*)**, which requires sensation and object to be one.
- **Act of awareness** (the **common** element) against **object of awareness** (the **variable** element). On the identity thesis the sensations of blue and green would share nothing and differ in nothing — both false.
- ✅ **The 2025 sentence:** "Blue is one object of sensation and green is another, and consciousness, which both sensations have in common, is different from either."
- ✅ **The 2020 sentence:** identifying "Blue exists" with "Both blue and consciousness exist" is a "mistake and a **self-contradictory** mistake" — the speaker must use the distinction he denies.
- **Transparency / diaphanousness:** a **diagnosis** of why the act is overlooked, not a premiss. ❓ Later philosophers of mind use the same observation against Moore.
- **Bounded result:** *esse est percipi* blocked ✅ · unperceived existence **not** proved ⚠️ · no theory of perception ⚠️ · **Absolute Idealism untouched** ✅ · Berkeley's positive system is Topic 03.
- **Moore against Russell on Berkeley:** Moore **blocks the argument** and leaves the analysis open; Russell **concedes the given** and rebuilds the physical world as a logical construction, breaking with Berkeley's nominalism by admitting acquaintance with universals.

### F. Russell — incomplete symbols and the theory of descriptions

- **Incomplete symbol:** no meaning **in isolation**; **contextual definition**; **disappears** on analysis. ❌ Not "meaningless". **Test:** does the expression survive analysis as a constituent?
- **"On Denoting", *Mind*, 1905.** **(Ex)[Fx & (Ay)(Fy -> y = x) & Gx]** — **existence · uniqueness · predication**.
- **Empty description → the sentence is FALSE**, not meaningless, and no Meinongian object subsists. The **golden mountain** is Meinong's own example.
- **Three puzzles:** negative existentials · excluded middle · substitutivity in attitude contexts (George IV and the author of *Waverley*).
- **Scope:** **primary occurrence** — description wide, "the King of France is not bald" is **false**; **secondary occurrence** — negation wide, it is **true**.
- ❓ **Strawson, "On Referring", 1950:** existence is **presupposed**, not asserted, so the truth question does not arise. A **rival analysis**, not a refutation; Topic 11 owns his positive theory.
- **The general principle established:** grammatical form is not logical form.

### G. Russell — acquaintance, description and logical constructions

- **Acquaintance:** direct, presentational, **non-inferential** awareness — sense-data, memory data, introspective data, and **universals** ⚠️ (a Platonic concession against Berkeley's nominalism).
- **Knowledge by description:** knowledge *that* exactly one thing is F, plus what is inferred — physical objects, other minds, historical persons, unobservables.
- ✅ **Principle of Acquaintance** (*Problems*, 1912, Ch. 5): "Every proposition which we can understand must be composed wholly of constituents with which we are acquainted."
- **Six-step argument:** understanding is constituent-grasping → I grasp only what I meet → yet I understand propositions about Bismarck → so either the Principle fails or the item is not a constituent → descriptions take the second route → the Principle is preserved. **Bismarck series:** himself · one who met him · one who read of him · "the most long-lived of men".
- **Logically proper names:** only "this" and "that", of what is presently given. ⚠️ Ordinary names are **disguised descriptions** — the part **Kripke** destroys (rigid designation).
- ✅ **Supreme maxim in scientific philosophising** (1914): "wherever possible, substitute constructions out of known entities for inferences to unknown entities."
- **Four constructions:** physical object ← classes of actual and possible sense-data · nation ← patterns of individual behaviour · number ← classes of equinumerous classes · point ← convergent classes of volumes.
- ❌ Constructions are **not fictions**; true things are said about tables and nations. **Strain:** "possible" sense-data are not straightforwardly known; solipsism worry; the base later becomes **neutral events** ⚠️ (a different phase, not a printed limb).
- ❓ **Sellars, 1956:** the Myth of the Given. **Verdict:** the logical machinery is permanent; the epistemology it served is not.

### H. Russell — logical atomism

- **1918 lectures**, published *The Monist* 1918–19; Russell's preface credits the ideas to **Wittgenstein**.
- **World = facts.** ⚠️ Facts are **truth-makers**, not truth-bearers; a list of objects does not describe the world.
- **Atomic fact:** contains **no other facts** as constituents — monadic (particular + quality) or relational (particulars + relation). ⚠️ It *does* have constituents; "atomic" is not "partless" and not "microscopic".
- ✅ **The 2020 sentence:** "the atoms that I wish to arrive at as the sort of **last residue in analysis** are **logical** atoms and not **physical** atoms."
- **Atomic ↔ molecular:** molecular propositions are **truth-functions** of atomic ones, so **no fact answers to "and" or "or"** — the economy that is the point of truth-functionality.
- **Isomorphism:** the true proposition and the fact share logical structure; names ↔ objects, arrangement ↔ arrangement.
- ⚠️ **Negative facts** accepted reluctantly, to preserve a direct truth-making relation; contested. Wittgenstein's **totality** account (2.05) with 4.0312 avoids them.
- ⚠️ **Descriptions support atomism without proving it:** no proof of termination, no identification of the simples, no exhaustive atomism. Assess atomism as a **programme**.

### I. Early Wittgenstein — ontology and the picture theory

- **1.1** "The world is the totality of facts, not of things." **1.13** the facts in logical space are the world. **2** a fact is the existence of states of affairs. **2.01** a state of affairs is a combination of objects. **2.013** possibility is built into the object. ⚠️ *Sachverhalt*: choose Ogden's "atomic fact" or Pears–McGuinness's "state of affairs" and stay with it.
- **Objects** are logical simples characterised by their possibilities of combination; ⚠️ **no uncontested example is given** — they are argued for, not exhibited.
- **Proposition = propositional sign + projective relation** (3.11–3.12). Elements ↔ objects; arrangement depicts arrangement. **True** if the state of affairs obtains, **false** if not, **meaningful either way** (2.201–2.224).
- **Pictorial form (*Form der Abbildung*)** — medium-specific: spatial models share spatial form, scores share acoustic form. **Logical form (*logische Form*)** — universal, the form of **all** representation (2.18); **every picture is a logical picture** (2.182).
- **How logical form defines the language–world relation:** it is **common** to proposition and fact, so the relation is **internal**, not an external convention or causal link.
- ⚠️ **Consequence:** logical form is not depictable; it **shows** itself (4.12–4.1212).
- ⚠️ "Picture theory" is the commentators' label. ❌ Never write "language is a picture of the world" without the **logical form** qualification.

### J. Early Wittgenstein — sense, logical space and the general form

- **Bipolarity:** sense requires capability of truth **and** of falsity. ⚠️ Stronger than **bivalence**. Anchors 4.06, 2.223, 2.224; the term is from *Notes on Logic* (1913).
- **Logical space:** 1.13, 2.11, **3.4** a proposition determines a place, 3.41, 3.42; **4.463** a proposition is "like a solid body that restricts the freedom of movement of others". To understand *p* is to understand ¬*p*.
- ✅ **4.0312, the *fundamental idea* (*Grundgedanke*):** "the logical constants are not representatives". Negation names nothing.
- **General form:** informal **4.5** "this is how things stand" ⚠️ (Ogden: "such and such is the case"); formal **6: [ p̄ , ξ̄ , N(ξ̄) ]**. **N = joint negation** (5.502, 5.51): ¬p = N(p); p v q = N(N(p,q)); p & q = N(N(p),N(q)). Quantification through sets of values of propositional functions (5.52) ⚠️ adequacy for infinite domains disputed.
- **What 6 asserts:** every proposition is a truth-function of elementary ones (5) → one operation generates them all → one general form → **logic needs no primitive propositions and no logical objects**, the break with Russell and Frege → "there can be no surprises in logic" (6.1251), logic's propositions are tautologies (6.1) → the **criterion of the sayable**.
- ⚠️ ***Sinnlos*** (tautology, contradiction — well formed, empty, showing logic's scaffolding, 4.461–4.4611) against ***unsinnig*** (metaphysical pseudo-proposition — depicting nothing). **The most tested discrimination in the *Tractatus*.**
- ⚠️ **Four presuppositions:** simples exist · elementary propositions are logically independent (2.061, 4.211) · all propositions are truth-functions (belief-contexts 5.54–5.542) · N handles infinite domains. **Colour exclusion** (1929) pressures the second.

### K. Early Wittgenstein — saying, showing and the ladder

- **Said (*gesagt*):** contingent states of affairs; the propositions of natural science (6.53). **Shown (*gezeigt*):** logical form; the existence of objects; logical truths and the scaffolding of the world (6.124); the language–world relation; **value**.
- ✅ **4.1212** "What can be shown, cannot be said."
- **The derivation, in five steps:** depiction requires shared logical form → depicting the sharing needs a standpoint outside logical form → every picture already has it → a regress threatens each attempted statement → therefore form **shows** itself.
- **The limit is drawn *in* language:** Preface; **5.6** "the limits of my language mean the limits of my world"; 5.61.
- ⚠️ **Solipsism, bounded:** 5.62 what the solipsist means is correct but cannot be said; 5.632–5.633 the subject is a **limit** of the world, and the eye is not in the visual field; 5.64 strict solipsism coincides with **pure realism**. Illustration only; the private-language critique is Topic 08.
- **Value:** 6.41 the sense of the world lies outside the world · 6.42 there can be no propositions of ethics · 6.421 ethics is transcendental · **6.522** "there is indeed the inexpressible; this shows itself; it is the mystical".
- **6.53** the correct method in philosophy. **6.54** the **ladder**. **7** — name the translation. ⚠️ **No 7.1.**
- ❓ **Standard ineffability** against **resolute/therapeutic** (Diamond, Conant). Both agree ethics and the mystical are not factual propositions. ❌ The *Tractatus* does not declare them **false**, and it contains **no verification principle**.

### L. Comparisons and the criticism ledger

- **Comparison axes:** target · core tool · ontology · metaphysics · self and subject · legacy. **Name the axis before comparing**, or the answer is two parallel descriptions.
- **The two atomisms.** *Atoms:* Russell — acquainted particulars plus qualities and relations; Wittgenstein — unspecified objects fixed by combinatorial possibility. *Ground:* Russell — logic plus a changing acquaintance epistemology; Wittgenstein — a condition-of-sense argument. *Independence:* loose against **strict**. *Negation:* **negative facts** against the **totality** account. *Expression:* 1918 lectures against the *Tractatus*. ⚠️ **Two atomisms with a common ancestry, not one doctrine.**
- **Criticism ledger, by residual.** **Moore** — question-begging; replies comparative and dialectical; **residual: no neutral measure of certainty**. **Russell** — Strawson's presupposition; the vanishing class of logically proper names; negative facts; **residual: no fixed inventory, and a genuine rival analysis of descriptions**. ***Tractatus*** — ethics not factual; self-application; no exemplified simples; colour exclusion; **residual: the standard/resolute split remains open**.
- **Three different failures.** Moore's gap is **epistemological**, Russell's is **ontological**, and Wittgenstein's concerns the **status of philosophical discourse** itself. Treating them as one failure is the commonest way of losing the assessment mark.

### M. Exam craft — directives, architecture, traps and the answer spine

- **Directive decoder:** descriptions questions demand **notation** · quotation questions demand that you **locate the sentence in its paper** · saying/showing questions demand the **derivation** · Moore questions demand the **structure of the reversal** · the 2020 atomism question demands that the **quoted phrases be decoded** · "how is X related to Y" demands **support, not entailment** · comparison demands an **axis**.
- **Architectures.** *10 marks:* problem → analysis → result → point. *15 marks:* frame → doctrine → quote → key move → result → assess. *20 marks:* frame → incomplete symbols → constructions → atomism → assess → close.
- **Ten-line trap sheet:** "incomplete symbol" is not "meaningless" · the empty description makes the sentence **false** · uniqueness is a conjunct, not decoration · *sinnlos* is not *unsinnig* · bipolarity is not bivalence · "atomic" is neither "physical" nor "partless" · facts are truth-makers, not truth-bearers · Moore refutes an **argument**, not idealism · the *Tractatus* has **no** verification principle · there is **no proposition 7.1**.
- ❌ **Never write:** that the *Tractatus* was published in English in 1921 · that Wittgenstein said language is a picture of the world without the logical-form qualification · that Russell invented logical atomism unaided · that Moore's naturalistic fallacy belongs to this clause · that Kripke's book appeared in 1970 and his lectures in 1980.
- **Memory chain:** the revolt against Absolute Idealism → Moore's truisms and the knowing/analysing distinction → comparative certainty and the two hands → the act/object distinction and transparency → incomplete symbols and the three conjuncts → acquaintance and the Principle → constructions and the supreme maxim → atomic facts, truth-functions and negative facts → facts, objects and the picture theory → bipolarity, logical space and the general form → saying, showing, the ladder and silence → comparison, criticism and craft.
- **Qualified conclusion.** ⚠️ The three programmes of this clause are one method used for three incompatible purposes, and each ends by leaving something deliberately unfinished. Moore protects ordinary knowledge by comparing certainties and leaves the analysis of that knowledge open; Russell secures ontological economy by exposing logical form and leaves the inventory of simples open; early Wittgenstein fixes the conditions of sense with a rigour neither of the others attempted and leaves the status of his own book open. Their permanent achievements are correspondingly precise: the burden of proof in epistemology was redistributed, the quantificational analysis of definite descriptions became a permanent instrument, and the account of sense as determinate exclusion within a field of possibilities set the agenda for a century of philosophy of language. Their permanent costs are equally precise: no neutral measure of certainty, no completed base, and no consistent way of stating the boundary the *Tractatus* draws. An answer that states an achievement and its matching cost in the same sentence has understood the clause; an answer that merges the three men has not.

   "There exists at present a living human body, which is my body."

THE TWO CLAIMS MADE ABOUT THE LIST
   [1] FIRST-ORDER  I KNOW these propositions - not believe, KNOW
   [2] SECOND-ORDER very many other human beings KNOW propositions of
       the SAME KIND about themselves
   -> the second converts a report of personal confidence into a claim
      about a SHARED epistemic position, and it is what makes "COMMON
      sense" the right name
        |
        v
WHAT THE DOCTRINE IS NOT
   NOT the claim that whatever most people believe is true
   NOT an appeal to convention
   NOT a claim of infallibility - the list is a philosophical proposal
       and is CONTESTABLE
        |
        v
WHY A LIST RATHER THAN AN ARGUMENT
   Moore does not argue FOR the truisms from something more basic -
   that would concede they need support from outside. He ASSERTS them,
   and the size of the list displays how enormous the sceptic's task is:
   he must deny not one claim but a whole interlocking body of them.

WHY MOORE NEEDED THE DISTINCTION - THE PARADOX OF ANALYSIS (bounded)
   If the analysis of "X is a brother" is "X is a male sibling", then
     EITHER it says the same thing -> trivially uninformative
     OR it says something different -> not an analysis of that concept
   NO SETTLED MOOREAN SOLUTION. Fregean sense / mode of presentation,
   and later accounts of concepts and synonymy, are PROPOSED responses.
   USE ONLY to show why analysis is not verbal substitution.
   OWNER STATUS: method-context, NOT a printed limb.

HONEST COST: the distinction protects knowledge only if propositional
   knowledge can be secure while its constituents are unclear - which
   a sceptic about the given, or anyone who ties understanding to
   analysis, will deny.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 7/28: Moore - comparative certainty and the reversal of the burden
==============================================================================
THE PRINCIPLE
   A philosophical argument overturns a truism ONLY IF every premiss of
   that argument is MORE CERTAIN than the truism it denies.
   Moore's empirical claim: NO SUCH PREMISS HAS EVER BEEN PRODUCED.
        |
        v
THE LOGIC BEHIND IT - a valid argument is a SET OF SCALES
   A valid argument transmits credibility BACKWARDS as well as forwards.
   If the conclusion is less credible than the premisses are credible,
   the argument has REFUTED A PREMISS rather than proved its conclusion.
        |
   THE SCEPTIC'S ARGUMENT              THE TRUISM
   P1 I cannot rule out dreaming       "I have a body."
   P2 if not, I do not know I have
      a body                           "The earth existed for many
   C  so I do not know                  years before I was born."
   abstract | technical | contested    as certain as anything I have
                                       ever been
        |
        v
RESULT: THE BURDEN OF PROOF IS REVERSED
   The realist no longer owes a proof of the external world from
   neutral ground; the SCEPTIC owes an explanation of why abstract
   premisses should be trusted more than ordinary perceptual certainty.
        |
        v
THE LIMIT THAT MUST BE WRITTEN
   The principle presupposes that certainty is COMPARABLE across
   ordinary and philosophical propositions, and Moore supplies NO
   NEUTRAL MEASURE. A sceptic who simply denies that Moore KNOWS the
   truism has been OUTWEIGHED, not shown to be irrational.
        |
        v
LABEL EXPLAINED (2019 Q3(c)): COMMON SENSE from the METHOD; REALISM
   from the CONTENT of the list - body, earth before my birth, other
   human beings. THE REALISM IS A RESULT, NOT A PREMISS.

MOORE'S OWN THREE CONDITIONS FOR A RIGOROUS PROOF
   [1] the premiss must be DIFFERENT from the conclusion
       "here is a hand" != "an external world exists" -> NOT CIRCULAR
   [2] the premiss must be KNOWN, not merely believed or assumed
       "I did know it. It would have been absurd to suggest that I did
        not know it, but only believed it." -> THE CONTESTED CONDITION
   [3] the conclusion must FOLLOW
       hands are things met with in space -> VALID, given the gloss
        |
        v
THE CONCESSION, AND WHY IT IS NOT A DEFECT
   Moore CANNOT PROVE the premiss - to do so he would have to prove he
   is not dreaming, "and that I cannot do".
   A proof requires a KNOWN premiss, not a PROVED one; the contrary
   demand generates an INFINITE REGRESS in which nothing is proved.
        |
        v
ANSWER LINE: "The demand to prove every premiss is a demand that no
   proof of anything could ever meet."

THE OBJECTION (and it is serious)
   Premiss 1 is exactly what the sceptic put in doubt. If dreaming or

   condition [2] fails, and the proof BEGS THE QUESTION.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 10/28: Moore 1903 - the target and the act/object distinction
==============================================================================
"THE REFUTATION OF IDEALISM", Mind, 1903
        |
        v
TARGET: ESSE EST PERCIPI - "to be is to be perceived"
   For the idealist inference to work, the object's BEING must be
   nothing over and above its BEING PERCEIVED. The sensation and its
   object must therefore be ONE THING, or the object a mere constituent
   of the act.
        |
        v
THE DISTINCTION
   +--------------------------------+-----------------------------+
   | ACT OF AWARENESS               | OBJECT OF AWARENESS         |
   | consciousness                  | blue, green, this sound     |
   | THE SAME in every sensation    | DIFFERENT in each sensation |
   | = THE COMMON ELEMENT           | = THE VARIABLE ELEMENT      |
   +--------------------------------+-----------------------------+
        |
        v
THE IDEALIST'S ERROR: conflating [1] and [2] - treating the object as
   IDENTICAL with, or CONSTITUTED BY, the act of awareness.
   If the sensation of blue IS just the blue, then blue cannot exist
   unperceived -> idealism.
        |
        v
WHY THE DISTINCTION IS NOT MERELY INTROSPECTIVE
   Moore does not simply report detecting two items. He argues that the
   identification makes EXPERIENCE UNINTELLIGIBLE: two sensations must
   have something IN COMMON (both are sensations) and must DIFFER in
   something (their objects differ). On the identity thesis there is
   only ONE item in each, so nothing is available to play both roles.
        |
        v
ANSWER LINE: "What varies cannot be what stays the same."

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 11/28: Moore - the two printed sentences, decoded
==============================================================================
THE 2025 SENTENCE (Q3(c))
   "Blue is one object of sensation and green is another, and
    consciousness, which both sensations have in common, is different
    from either."
   READ IT AS A LABELLING OF THE ARGUMENT:
     "blue ... green" ............ THE VARIABLE ELEMENT = the OBJECTS
     "which both sensations have in common" .... THE COMMON ELEMENT =
        consciousness
     "is different from either" .. THE CONCLUSION: act != object
        |
        v
THE 2020 SENTENCE (Q2(b))
   "If anyone tells us that to say 'Blue exists' is the same thing as
    to say that 'Both blue and consciousness exist', he makes a mistake
    and a SELF-CONTRADICTORY mistake."
        |
   WHY SELF-CONTRADICTORY - four steps, and this decoding is the mark
   [1] the claim is that the two statements say the SAME THING
   [2] the second statement is formed by CONJOINING TWO DISTINCT ITEMS,
       blue and consciousness
   [3] to assert the identity of content, the speaker must already
       POSSESS AND USE the very distinction whose significance he denies
   [4] therefore the identification is not merely false; it CANNOT BE
       STATED without presupposing its own falsity
        |
        v
THE RESIDUAL - state it, and the "critically" half is done
   The charge bites hardest against a strict IDENTITY formulation. An
   idealist who says instead that blue is DEPENDENT ON consciousness
   without being IDENTICAL with it has not been convicted of
   contradiction - he has been DEPRIVED OF THE ARGUMENT HE HAD.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 12/28: Moore - transparency, and the exact boundary of the result
==============================================================================
TRANSPARENCY / DIAPHANOUSNESS
   Introspect the sensation of blue and what you find is THE BLUE. The
   act is elusive - "as if it were diaphanous". Attention passes
   THROUGH the act to the object.
   ROLE: a DIAGNOSIS of why the conflation is tempting.
   NOT A PREMISS of the refutation - the comparison argument does the
   refuting.
   CONTESTED: later philosophers of mind use the same observation to
   argue that there is NOTHING beyond the object to introspect. Moore's
   reply must be that ELUSIVENESS IS NOT ABSENCE.
        |
        v
THE BOUNDARY - the half of the answer most scripts omit
   ACHIEVED ...... the IDENTITY route from object to act is BLOCKED, so
                   esse est percipi is NOT ESTABLISHED
   NOT ACHIEVED .. that any object exists UNPERCEIVED
   NOT ACHIEVED .. a positive THEORY OF PERCEPTION - the relation
                   between sense-data and material objects is left open
   NOT ACHIEVED .. a refutation of ABSOLUTE IDEALISM, which rests on
                   internal relations and the incompleteness of finite
                   determinations, NOT on the perceptual equation
   NOT ACHIEVED .. a refutation of Berkeley's whole philosophy - his
                   positive system is TOPIC 03
        |
        v
MOORE vs RUSSELL ON BERKELEY (the comparative half of 2018 Q3(a))
   MOORE     epistemic and DISTINCTION-DRAWING: denies the
             identification, protects ordinary certainty, offers NO
             replacement theory of perception
   RUSSELL   logical and RECONSTRUCTIVE: accepts that what is given is
             sense-data, rebuilds physical objects as LOGICAL
             CONSTRUCTIONS, removes apparent names by CONTEXTUAL
             ANALYSIS, and breaks with Berkeley's NOMINALISM by
             admitting acquaintance with UNIVERSALS
   ONE-SENTENCE DIFFERENCE: Moore blocks the argument and leaves the
   analysis open; Russell concedes the starting point and supplies a
   replacement architecture.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 13/28: Russell - incomplete symbols and contextual definition
==============================================================================
DEFINITION
   An INCOMPLETE SYMBOL has NO MEANING IN ISOLATION, contributes
   systematically to the meaning of every sentence in which it occurs,
   and DISAPPEARS on analysis. It is given a CONTEXTUAL DEFINITION -
   a rule supplying the meaning of WHOLE SENTENCES containing it.
   SOURCE OF THE TECHNIQUE: contextual definition in Principia
   Mathematica (1910-13).
        |
        v
THE TEST TO APPLY IN THE EXAM
   Does the expression SURVIVE ANALYSIS as a CONSTITUENT of the
   proposition?
     "the author of Waverley" ....... does NOT survive -> incomplete
     "Scott", as a genuine name ..... would survive
        |
        v
THE COMMONEST ERROR
   "INCOMPLETE" DOES NOT MEAN "MEANINGLESS". The phrase is
   systematically meaning-CONTRIBUTING; it has no meaning ON ITS OWN.
   Write the words "in isolation" and the error cannot occur.
        |
        v
CONSEQUENCE FOR ONTOLOGY
   Because the phrase contributes NO CONSTITUENT, the truth of a
   sentence containing it commits us to nothing beyond the existence of
   things satisfying the PREDICATES. This is what makes the device an
   instrument of ONTOLOGICAL ECONOMY, and it is the bridge to
   CONSTRUCTIONS (panel 19) and ATOMISM (panels 20-22).
        |
        v
OPTIONAL BACKGROUND ONLY: Russell's PARADOX and the THEORY OF TYPES
   supply the habit behind the doctrine - a symbol may be ELIMINABLE
   rather than referential, and a well-formed-looking string may fail to
   express a proposition. NOT a printed limb. One sentence maximum.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 14/28: Russell - the theory of descriptions, in notation
==============================================================================
THE PROBLEM: "The present King of France is bald."
   Grammatically impeccable. Understood by every competent speaker.
   And there is no present King of France.
        |
   THREE ANSWERS RUSSELL REJECTS
   (a) THE SENTENCE IS MEANINGLESS ..... but we understand it, and we
       can argue about it
   (b) IT IS ABOUT A NON-EXISTENT OBJECT THAT SUBSISTS ..... MEINONG's
       route; it populates the world with golden mountains, and the
       ROUND SQUARE threatens non-contradiction
   (c) THE PHRASE DENOTES SOMETHING ELSE ..... no candidate survives
        |
        v
THE ANALYSIS - the phrase is ELIMINATED, not interpreted
        "The F is G"
             =
   (Ex)[ Fx  AND  (Ay)(Fy -> y = x)  AND  Gx ]
          ^             ^                   ^
      EXISTENCE     UNIQUENESS         PREDICATION
     at least one    at most one          it is G
        |
        v
WORKED CASE - "THE GOLDEN MOUNTAIN IS VERY HIGH" (2025 Q1(d))
   (Ex)[ Gx & (Ay)(Gy -> y=x) & Hx ]
   The EXISTENCE conjunct is FALSE -> the whole conjunction is FALSE
   -> NOT meaningless, and NO golden mountain need subsist
   The golden mountain is MEINONG'S OWN STOCK EXAMPLE - name him.
        |
        v
UNIQUENESS IS THE CONJUNCT MOST OFTEN DROPPED. Without it the analysis
   renders "the" as "a", and the definite article's entire contribution
   disappears.
        |
        v
THE GENERAL PRINCIPLE ESTABLISHED
   GRAMMATICAL FORM != LOGICAL FORM. The task of philosophy is to
   reveal the logical form hidden by language.

THE ADVERTISEMENT: ONE DEVICE, THREE INDEPENDENT PROBLEMS. A theory

NOT ONE OF THE THREE: the problem of UNIVERSALS. That is a separate

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 16/28: Russell - scope, and the Strawson objection
==============================================================================
PRIMARY AND SECONDARY OCCURRENCE - "The King of France is not bald"
   PRIMARY (description takes WIDE scope):
      there is exactly one King of France AND he is not bald -> FALSE
   SECONDARY (negation takes WIDE scope):
      it is NOT the case that (there is exactly one King of France and
      he is bald) -> TRUE
   ONE sentence, TWO logical forms. Ambiguity of SCOPE, not of the
   meanings of the words.
        |
        v
THE STANDING OBJECTION
   STRAWSON, "On Referring", Mind, 1950
   Russell has confused a SENTENCE with a USE of a sentence. A speaker
   who says "The present King of France is bald" does not ASSERT that
   there is exactly one King of France; he PRESUPPOSES it. When the
   presupposition fails, THE QUESTION OF TRUTH OR FALSITY DOES NOT
   ARISE - the utterance is not false but VOID.
        |
        v
RUSSELL'S AVAILABLE REPLIES
   [1] Strawson describes conversational expectation; Russell gives
       TRUTH-CONDITIONS, and a logic requires them
   [2] presupposition failure needs a semantics of its own - typically
       a TRUTH-VALUE GAP - and gaps carry costs for classical logic
   [3] the analysis is a proposal about LOGICAL FORM for a scientifically
       regimented language, not ordinary-language lexicography
        |
        v
THE HONEST VERDICT
   A GENUINE RIVAL ANALYSIS, not a demonstration of error. Strawson
   describes ordinary USE better; Russell describes LOGICAL FORM better.
   OWNER BOUNDARY: Strawson appears here as ONE OBJECTION. His positive
   theory of reference and his descriptive metaphysics are TOPIC 11.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 17/28: Russell - acquaintance, description and the Principle
==============================================================================
TWO KINDS OF KNOWLEDGE OF THINGS
   ACQUAINTANCE   direct, presentational, NON-INFERENTIAL - "directly
                  aware... without the intermediary of any process of
                  inference"
                  OBJECTS: sense-data | memory data | introspective
                  data of one's own mental states | UNIVERSALS
   DESCRIPTION    indirect, propositional - knowledge THAT there is
                  exactly one thing answering to "the F", plus what is
                  inferred of it
                  OBJECTS: physical objects | other minds | historical
                  persons | unobservables of science
        |
   THE PLATONIC CONCESSION: acquaintance with UNIVERSALS is a large
   admission for a philosopher in the British empiricist line, and
   Russell makes it openly AGAINST Berkeley's nominalism.
        |
        v
THE PRINCIPLE OF ACQUAINTANCE (Problems of Philosophy, 1912, Ch. 5)
   "Every proposition which we can understand must be composed wholly
    of constituents with which we are acquainted."
   IT CONSTRAINS UNDERSTANDING, NOT TRUTH.
        |
        v
THE ARGUMENT, NUMBERED
   1 to understand a proposition is to grasp its constituents
   2 I can grasp only what I am acquainted with
   3 yet I plainly understand propositions about Bismarck, Julius
     Caesar and electrons
   4 so EITHER the Principle is false OR those items are NOT
     constituents of the propositions
   5 THE THEORY OF DESCRIPTIONS takes the second route: "the F is G"
     contains NO constituent "the F"; its constituents are the
     UNIVERSALS F and G plus logical apparatus - all acquaintance items
   6 therefore the Principle is PRESERVED
        |
        v
THE BISMARCK SERIES (Russell's own, graded)
   BISMARCK HIMSELF ......... acquaintance with his own self-
                              consciousness
   ONE WHO MET HIM .......... acquaintance with sense-data he took to be
                              of the man; the description is anchored to
                              HIS particulars
   ONE WHO READ OF HIM ...... purely descriptive: "the first Chancellor
                              of the German Empire"
   ALL OF US, OF "THE MOST
     LONG-LIVED OF MEN" ..... a description whose satisfier we may never
                              identify - and the proposition is FULLY
                              UNDERSTOOD
        |
        v
ANSWER LINE: "The theory of descriptions is the price Russell pays to
   keep the Principle of Acquaintance while speaking of what he has
   never met."

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 18/28: Russell - logically proper names, and the two modern objections
==============================================================================
LOGICALLY PROPER NAMES
   A symbol that DIRECTLY DESIGNATES an item of acquaintance and could
   not fail to designate.
   In Russell's austere phase the only serious candidates are the
   DEMONSTRATIVES "this" and "that", used of something PRESENTLY GIVEN.
        |
   TWO CONSEQUENCES, BOTH EXAMINABLE
   [1] ORDINARY NAMES - "Bismarck", "Socrates", "Homer" - are DISGUISED
       DEFINITE DESCRIPTIONS, analysed by the theory of descriptions
   [2] the class of genuine names shrinks ALMOST TO NOTHING, and it
       shrinks DIFFERENTLY for each speaker and each moment
        |
        v
   +----------------------------------+----------------------------+
   | KRIPKE, Naming and Necessity     | SELLARS, "Empiricism and   |
   | lectures 1970, book 1980         | the Philosophy of Mind",   |
   |                                  | 1956                       |
   +----------------------------------+----------------------------+
   | Ordinary proper names are NOT    | THE MYTH OF THE GIVEN:     |
   | disguised descriptions.          | nothing is given           |
   | "Aristotle" would still name     | uninterpreted, and what is |
   | Aristotle even if every          | given non-conceptually     |
   | description we associate with    | cannot JUSTIFY beliefs.    |
   | him were false. Names are RIGID  |                            |
   | DESIGNATORS.                     | RUSSELL'S REPLY:           |
   |                                  | acquaintance is            |
   | RUSSELL'S RETREAT: confine the   | PRESENTATION, not          |
   | claim to LOGICALLY proper names  | knowledge of truths.       |
   | and abandon the description      |                            |
   | theory of ORDINARY names.        | RESIDUAL: then how does it |
   |                                  | GROUND anything? THE LIVE  |
   | STANDARD VERDICT: the LOGIC of   | WEAKNESS.                  |
   | descriptions survives; the       |                            |
   | THEORY OF NAMES does not.        |                            |
   +----------------------------------+----------------------------+
        |
        v
KEEP THEM APART: Kripke attacks the theory of NAMES; Sellars attacks
   the BASE. Different targets, different residues - and Russell can
   survive one without surviving the other.
        |
        v
PHASE WARNING: sense-data acquaintance | the 1918 atomism | later
   NEUTRAL MONISM are THREE POSITIONS at three times. Do not present
   them as one timeless doctrine.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 19/28: Russell - logical constructions and the supreme maxim
==============================================================================
THE SUPREME MAXIM IN SCIENTIFIC PHILOSOPHISING (1914)
   "Wherever possible, substitute constructions out of known entities
    for inferences to unknown entities."
   SOURCES: "The Relation of Sense-Data to Physics"; Our Knowledge of
   the External World. Restated in "Logical Atomism" (1924).
        |
   THE CHOICE IT FORCES
   ROUTE A  INFER a table BEHIND the appearances, never itself met
            COST: an entity I can never check, doing explanatory work
            I cannot inspect
   ROUTE B  DEFINE "the table" as an ordered system of ACTUAL and
            POSSIBLE appearances from various viewpoints
            COST: the system must really be specifiable
        |
        v
FOUR WORKED CONSTRUCTIONS - learn at least two of DIFFERENT types
   a physical object ("this table") <- classes of ACTUAL and POSSIBLE
        sense-data from various points of view
   a "nation" <- patterns of behaviour of individual persons
   a number <- classes of equinumerous classes (Frege-Russell)
   a point of space <- convergent classes of volumes
   THE SPREAD (perceptual | social | mathematical | geometrical) is the
   evidence that the maxim is a METHOD, not a repair to one problem.
        |
        v
CONSTRUCTIONS vs ATOMS
   ATOMS ........... genuinely exist as independent simples within the
                     analysis; the RESIDUE analysis SEEKS
   CONSTRUCTIONS ... add NO further entity; their SYMBOLS are defined
                     CONTEXTUALLY; what analysis REMOVES
        |
        v
THREE THINGS A CONSTRUCTION IS NOT
   NOT a fiction - we say TRUE things about tables and nations
   NOT an elimination of ordinary talk - the talk is preserved and
       given truth-conditions
   NOT a permanent phenomenalist metaphysics - a PROPOSED reduction /
       structural reconstruction, and Russell's base later became
       NEUTRAL EVENTS
        |
        v
THE STRAIN
   The construction needs POSSIBLE sense-data ("sensibilia") from
   viewpoints no one occupies. These are not straightforwardly KNOWN
   entities, so the inference the maxim was meant to avoid may have
   been smuggled back. And a private-sense-data base invites SOLIPSISM;
   Russell's reply is that constructions are PUBLIC-THEORETICAL
   reconstructions aimed at explanatory economy.
        |
        v
VERDICT: the TECHNIQUE is permanent; the 1914 PHENOMENALIST INVENTORY
   is a phase. Distinguishing the two IS what "critically discuss" asks.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 20/28: Russell - logical atomism, and the 2020 sentence decoded
==============================================================================
"THE PHILOSOPHY OF LOGICAL ATOMISM" - lectures 1918, London; published
   in The Monist 1918-19. THE PREFACE CREDITS THE IDEAS TO WITTGENSTEIN.
        |
        v
THE 2020 QUOTATION, DECODED CLAUSE BY CLAUSE (Q2(c))
   "The reason that I call my doctrine logical atomism is because the
    atoms that I wish to arrive at as the sort of last residue in
    analysis are logical atoms and not physical atoms."
   "last residue in ANALYSIS" .. atomism is defined by a METHOD: what
        survives when analysis has removed every complexity
   "LOGICAL ... NOT PHYSICAL" .. the atoms are constituents of FACTS -
        particulars, qualities, relations - not tiny bodies
   "my DOCTRINE" ............... an ONTOLOGY read off a LOGIC, not a
        physics
        |
        v
WORLD = FACTS, NOT THINGS
   A FACT is what makes a proposition true or false.
   FACTS ARE NOT THEMSELVES TRUE OR FALSE - they are TRUTH-MAKERS;
   propositions are TRUTH-BEARERS.
   A complete catalogue of OBJECTS would still leave undecided which of
   them have which qualities and how they are related. Only FACTS
   settle that.
        |
        v
THE STRUCTURE OF AN ATOMIC FACT
   MONADIC ....... a PARTICULAR exemplifies a QUALITY   "this is red"
   RELATIONAL .... PARTICULARS stand in a RELATION
                   "this is to the left of that"
   "ATOMIC" = CONTAINS NO OTHER FACTS AS CONSTITUENTS.
   It PLAINLY HAS constituents. Reading "atomic" as "partless" is as
   damaging as reading it as "microscopic".
        |
        v
POINT OF CONTACT WITH THE TRACTATUS: 1.1 makes the same move - "the
   world is the totality of facts, not of things". BUT the two men
   agree that the world is FACTUAL and disagree about what the
   constituents of facts are and how we know them. See panel 28.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 21/28: Russell - truth-functions, isomorphism and negative facts
==============================================================================
THE TWO-STOREY BUILDING
   UPPER: MOLECULAR PROPOSITIONS
     "this is red AND that is blue" | "... OR ..." | "NOT ..." |
     "IF ... THEN ..."
     TRUTH-VALUE fixed ENTIRELY by the atoms below plus the connective
   GROUND: ATOMIC PROPOSITIONS
     "this is red" | "this is to the left of that" - no connectives
   FOUNDATION: ATOMIC FACTS
        |
        v
THE PAY-OFF OF TRUTH-FUNCTIONALITY
   NO ADDITIONAL FACT IN THE WORLD CORRESPONDS TO "and" OR "or".
   There is no conjunctive fact over and above the two conjuncts'
   facts. A large ONTOLOGICAL ECONOMY - state it, or you have
   described a truth-table instead of an argument.
        |
        v
THE ISOMORPHISM THESIS
   PROPOSITION   name1 ---- relation-word ---- name2
                   |              |              |
   FACT          object1 ------ relation ----- object2
   SAME LOGICAL STRUCTURE. Names correspond to objects; the ARRANGEMENT
   of names corresponds to the ARRANGEMENT of objects. This is the
   BRIDGE between language and world in Russell's atomism.
        |
        v
THE NEGATIVE-FACT PROBLEM
   "Socrates is not alive" is TRUE. What makes it true?
   RUSSELL: reluctantly, a NEGATIVE FACT - that a positive state does
     NOT obtain.
   COST: absences are added to the inventory of the world.
   WHY HE PAYS IT: to preserve a DIRECT TRUTH-MAKING relation - every
     truth is made true by something.
   WITTGENSTEIN'S DIFFERENT ROUTE: obtaining elementary states
     constitute the world, and their TOTALITY also determines which
     possible states do NOT obtain (2.05); negation is not a further
     object, and "the logical constants are not representatives"
     (4.0312). NO negative facts required.
        |
        v
THE EXPOSED ASSUMPTION
   The programme requires that EVERY proposition be a truth-function of
   atomic ones. BELIEF-CONTEXTS ("George IV believes that p") and
   MODAL contexts resist this. Concede the difficulty; do not pretend
   the reduction is complete.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 22/28: Russell - how far descriptions support atomism
==============================================================================
THE QUESTION OF 2018 Q4(c) AND 2024 Q3(a): how is the theory of
   descriptions RELATED to logical atomism?
        |
        v
WHAT DESCRIPTIONS DO SUPPLY
   [1] contextual analysis removes MERELY APPARENT CONSTITUENTS -
       "the F" is shown not to be a constituent at all
   [2] what remains is QUANTIFIED STRUCTURE plus GENUINE LOGICAL
       CONSTITUENTS
   [3] that is exactly the discrimination atomism needs: real residues
       separated from grammatical illusions
   [4] methodological continuity - both rest on GRAMMATICAL FORM !=
       LOGICAL FORM. Descriptions show the principle has teeth;
       atomism is the ontology reached by applying it systematically
        |
        v
WHAT DESCRIPTIONS DO NOT SUPPLY
   [1] NO proof that analysis TERMINATES
   [2] NO identification of the SIMPLES
   [3] NO demonstration that reality is EXHAUSTIVELY atomic
   Removing one class of pseudo-constituents is not reaching a base.
        |
        v
THE VERDICT SENTENCE (write it)
   "The theory of descriptions makes logical atomism REASONABLE and
    does not make it PROVED: it shows how a residue is reached in a
    particular case, and leaves open whether every analysis reaches
    one."
        |
        v
THE FOUR-STAGE ROUTE FOR THE 20-MARK ANSWER
   ACQUAINTANCE fixes what is directly available
        -> INCOMPLETE SYMBOLS supply the technique of elimination
        -> LOGICAL CONSTRUCTIONS apply it to the entities of common
           sense and science
        -> LOGICAL ATOMISM names the residue analysis expects to find
   CONNECTING SENTENCE: "Incomplete symbols supply the scalpel,
   constructions are the operations performed with it, and atomism is
   the anatomy the surgeon expects to find - which is why Russell can
   be confident in the technique long before he is confident about the
   inventory."

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 23/28: The Tractatus - facts, objects and states of affairs
==============================================================================
THE OPENING ONTOLOGY
   1     The world is all that is the case.
   1.1   The world is the totality of FACTS, not of THINGS.
   1.13  The facts in LOGICAL SPACE are the world.
   2     What is the case, a fact, is the existence of STATES OF
         AFFAIRS.
   2.01  A state of affairs is a COMBINATION OF OBJECTS.
   2.013 Each thing is, as it were, in a SPACE of possible states of
         affairs - POSSIBILITY IS BUILT INTO THE OBJECT.
   2.06  The existence and non-existence of states of affairs is
         reality.
        |
        v
WHY FACTS AND NOT THINGS
   A complete inventory of objects leaves everything undecided: it does
   not tell you which qualities they have or how they are related. What
   is the case is a matter of CONFIGURATION, and a configuration is a
   FACT.
        |
        v
OBJECTS
   Logical SIMPLES, characterised NOT by an intrinsic description but by
   the STATES OF AFFAIRS INTO WHICH THEY CAN ENTER (2.0121, 2.013).
   The space of possible configurations is fixed once the objects are.
   THE HONEST GAP: the text gives NO UNCONTESTED EXAMPLE of a simple
   object or an elementary proposition. It argues that simples are
   REQUIRED for determinate sense; it does not EXHIBIT them.
        |
        v
TRANSLATION CONTROL
   Sachverhalt = "atomic fact" (Ogden/Ramsey) = "state of affairs"
   (Pears-McGuinness). CHOOSE ONE AND STAY WITH IT.
        |
        v
CONTRAST WITH RUSSELL - do not merge
   RUSSELL'S atoms are identified EPISTEMICALLY, through acquaintance,
   in the 1918 phase.
   WITTGENSTEIN'S objects are POSTULATED by a CONDITION-OF-SENSE
   argument and are never identified.
   TWO ATOMISMS, ONE WORD.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 24/28: The Tractatus - the picture theory, and the two forms
==============================================================================
THE PICTURING RELATION - three requirements
   (i)   ELEMENTS of the picture (names) correspond to OBJECTS
   (ii)  the ARRANGEMENT of the elements depicts an arrangement of the
         objects
   (iii) a METHOD OF PROJECTION connects sign to situation: the
         propositional SIGN plus its PROJECTIVE RELATION to the world
         is the PROPOSITION (3.11-3.12)
   TRUE  if the depicted state of affairs obtains
   FALSE if it does not
   MEANINGFUL EITHER WAY, because a POSSIBLE arrangement is presented
   (2.201-2.203, 2.221-2.224)
   2.223 "to tell whether a picture is true or false we must compare it
         with reality"
   2.224 "it is impossible to tell from the picture alone whether it is
         true or false"
        |
        v
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
   | PICTORIAL FORM            | LOGICAL FORM                    |
   | (Form der Abbildung)      | (logische Form)                 |
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
   | what a picture must share | the MOST ABSTRACT structural    |
   | with reality to depict it | commonality - what EVERY        |
   | AT ALL, IN ITS OWN MEDIUM | picture, in ANY medium, must    |
   |                           | share with reality (2.18)       |
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
   | a SPATIAL model shares    | every meaningful proposition    |
   | spatial form; a SCORE     | shares LOGICAL form with the    |
   | shares acoustic form      | fact it depicts                 |
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
   | a picture can depict only | a proposition can depict ANY    |
   | what shares ITS form      | fact whatever - logical form is |
   |                           | the UNIVERSAL medium            |
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
   RELATION: logical form is what the pictorial forms HAVE IN COMMON.
   EVERY picture is a LOGICAL picture (2.182); not every picture is
   spatial or musical.
        |
        v
HOW LOGICAL FORM DEFINES THE LANGUAGE-WORLD RELATION (2022 Q3(c),
   second half - do not omit it)
   Because logical form is COMMON to proposition and fact, language and
   world are not two realms needing a bridge. The relation is INTERNAL -
   constitutive of the proposition's being a picture at all - not an
   external correlation fixed by convention or causal contact.
        |
        v
PROVENANCE: "picture theory" is the COMMENTATORS' label. Wittgenstein
   writes Bild (picture/model) and abbilden (to depict). Say "the
   doctrine usually called the picture theory".
CONSEQUENCE: logical form is NOT a depictable fact - any depiction
   would presuppose it. IT SHOWS ITSELF (4.12, 4.121, 4.1212).

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 25/28: The Tractatus - bipolarity, logical space and the Grundgedanke
==============================================================================
BIPOLARITY - THE CONDITION OF SENSE
   A proposition has SENSE (Sinn) only if it CAN be true and CAN be
   false. STRONGER than bivalence, which says only that it HAS one of
   two values.
   ANCHORS 4.06 | 2.223 | 2.224; the TERM comes from Notes on Logic
   (1913).
   WHY IT DOES SO MUCH WORK
     1 to depict is to represent something AS being the case
     2 to represent something as being the case is to allow that it
       might NOT be the case
     3 therefore sense requires the genuine availability of BOTH
       truth-values
        |
        v
LOGICAL SPACE - WHERE THE POSSIBILITIES LIVE
   1.13 the facts in logical space are the world
   2.11 a picture presents a situation in logical space
   3.4  a proposition DETERMINES A PLACE in logical space
   3.41 the propositional sign and the logical co-ordinates: that is
        the logical place
   3.42 the existence of the logical place is guaranteed by the mere
        existence of the constituent parts
   4.463 a proposition "is like a solid body that restricts the freedom
        of movement of others" - A PROPOSITION IS A LIMIT ON HOW THE
        WORLD MAY BE
   ANALOGY: logical space is to propositions what a COORDINATE SYSTEM
   is to points - to specify a point you need the whole grid.
        |
        v
FOUR CONSEQUENCES
   SENSE IS HOLISTIC ....... to understand p is to understand not-p:
                             the same logical place, opposite pole
   NEGATION IS NOT AN OBJECT 4.0312, the GRUNDGEDANKE: "my fundamental
                             idea is that the 'logical constants' are
                             not representatives"
   THE WORLD IS DETERMINATE  2.013 - possibility is built into the
                             object, not added to it
   LIMITS OF LANGUAGE =
     LIMITS OF THE WORLD ... 5.6 - one cannot think outside logical
                             space, so its bounds cannot be described
                             from outside
        |
        v
ANSWER LINE: "If the world could not refute it, it did not tell you
   anything."

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 26/28: The Tractatus - general form, N-operator, two failures
==============================================================================
THE GENERAL FORM OF THE PROPOSITION - give BOTH formulations
   (a) INFORMAL, 4.5: "THIS IS HOW THINGS STAND"
       (Es verhaelt sich so und so). OGDEN: "Such and such is the
       case". FLAG THE RENDERING.
   (b) FORMAL, 6:        [ p , x , N(x) ]
         p (p-bar) .... the totality of ELEMENTARY propositions
         x (xi-bar) ... any arbitrarily selected SET of propositions
                        already formed
         N(x) ......... the result of applying the N-OPERATOR
        |
   THE N-OPERATOR (5.502, 5.51) = JOINT NEGATION: none of the selected
   propositions is true.
        not-p   = N(p)
        p or q  = N(N(p,q))
        p and q = N(N(p), N(q))
   Quantification through sets of values of propositional functions
   (5.52); adequacy for INFINITE or MULTIPLY GENERAL cases is DISPUTED.
        |
        v
WHAT PROPOSITION 6 ASSERTS
   1 every proposition is a TRUTH-FUNCTION of elementary propositions(5)
   2 every truth-function is producible by repeated application of ONE
     operation, N
   3 so there is ONE general form - that of a FORMAL SERIES generated
     from the elementary base
   4 so LOGIC NEEDS NO PRIMITIVE PROPOSITIONS AND NO LOGICAL OBJECTS -
     the DECISIVE BREAK with Russell and Frege
   5 so "there can be no surprises in logic" (6.1251), and logic's
     propositions are TAUTOLOGIES (6.1)
   6 so whatever cannot be produced by this rule IS NOT A PROPOSITION -
     the general form is the CRITERION OF THE SAYABLE
        |
        v
THE TWO WAYS OF FAILING TO SAY ANYTHING
   SINNLOS (senseless, but WELL FORMED)
     TAUTOLOGY ..... true under every truth-condition; excludes NOTHING
                     "it is raining or it is not raining"
     CONTRADICTION . false under every truth-condition; excludes
                     EVERYTHING
     They belong to the symbolism and SHOW the scaffolding of logic
     (4.461-4.4611, 6.124). NOT nonsensical.
   UNSINNIG (nonsensical)
     "The Absolute is perfect"; "There are objects"
     Depicts NO possible situation; never entered logical space at all.
   THIS IS THE MOST TESTED DISCRIMINATION IN THE TRACTATUS.
        |
        v
FOUR PRESUPPOSITIONS - state as PRESSURES, not as conceded refutations
   [1] there ARE elementary propositions and simple objects - no
       uncontested example is given
   [2] elementary propositions are LOGICALLY INDEPENDENT (2.061, 4.211)
       - COLOUR EXCLUSION pressures this: "this patch is red all over"
       and "this patch is blue all over" are incompatible although
       neither looks like a truth-function of the other. Wittgenstein
       addressed it in "Some Remarks on Logical Form" (1929) and
       quickly abandoned the proposed analysis.
   [3] ALL propositions are truth-functions of elementary ones -
       belief-contexts (5.54-5.542) and modality create difficulties
   [4] the N-operator can handle INFINITE domains - disputed
   BOUNDARY: colour exclusion diagnoses a pressure; it does NOT license
   importing MEANING AS USE, which is TOPIC 08.

ASCII MASTER FLOW — PANEL 27/28: The Tractatus - saying, showing, the limit, the ladder and 7
==============================================================================
THE DERIVATION - why the distinction is FORCED, not stipulated
   1 a proposition depicts by SHARING logical form with what it depicts
   2 to DEPICT that sharing, a further picture would have to stand
     OUTSIDE logical form and represent it as one fact among others
   3 but any picture whatever ALREADY HAS logical form - there is no
     representation from outside representation
   4 a REGRESS also threatens: each attempted statement of form would
     need its own form stated
   5 THEREFORE logical form is NOT SAYABLE. It SHOWS ITSELF in every
     proposition that has it (4.12, 4.121, 4.1212)
   4.1212 "WHAT CAN BE SHOWN, CANNOT BE SAID."
        |
        v
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
   | CAN BE SAID (gesagt)      | CAN ONLY BE SHOWN (gezeigt)     |
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
   | propositions of NATURAL   | LOGICAL FORM                    |
   | SCIENCE (6.53)            | THE EXISTENCE OF OBJECTS        |
   | contingent states of      | LOGICAL TRUTHS - the scaffolding|
   | affairs                   |   of the world (6.124)          |
   |                           | ETHICS, AESTHETICS, THE MEANING |
   |                           |   OF LIFE, THE MYSTICAL         |
   |                           | THE LANGUAGE-WORLD RELATION     |
   +---------------------------+---------------------------------+
        |
        v
VALUE - the exact propositions
   6.41  the sense of the world must lie OUTSIDE the world; in the
         world everything is as it is, and in it there is NO VALUE
   6.42  there can be NO PROPOSITIONS OF ETHICS
   6.421 ethics cannot be put into words; ethics is TRANSCENDENTAL
   6.522 "there is indeed the inexpressible; this SHOWS itself; it is
         the MYSTICAL"
   NOT A DISMISSAL: ethical claims are NOT declared FALSE and NOT
   declared unimportant. A DIFFERENT VERDICT, and a more respectful one.
        |
        v
THE LIMIT IS DRAWN FROM INSIDE
   PREFACE: the limit "can only be drawn in language, and what lies on
     the other side of the limit will simply be nonsense"
   5.6  the limits of my language mean the limits of my world
   5.61 we cannot say what we cannot think, so we cannot say what we
     cannot say
   SOLIPSISM, BOUNDED ORIENTATION ONLY
     5.62 "what the solipsist MEANS is quite correct, only it cannot be
       SAID: it shows itself"
     5.632-5.633 the subject does not belong to the world; it is a
       LIMIT of the world - THE EYE IS NOT IN THE VISUAL FIELD
     5.64 strictly maintained, solipsism COINCIDES WITH PURE REALISM
     USE TO ILLUSTRATE THE BOUNDARY. The private-language critique is
     TOPIC 08.
        |
        v
SELF-APPLICATION, THE LADDER AND 7
   Every sentence of the Tractatus is about logical form, objects and
   the limits of language. BY ITS OWN TEST NONE SAYS ANYTHING.
   6.54 (Pears-McGuinness): "anyone who understands me eventually
     recognises them as nonsensical, when he has used them - as steps -
     to climb beyond them. (He must, so to speak, throw away the ladder
     after he has climbed it.)"
   7  OGDEN: "Whereof one cannot speak, thereof one must be silent."
      PEARS-McGUINNESS: "What we cannot speak about we must pass over
      in silence."
      NAME THE TRANSLATION. THERE IS NO PROPOSITION 7.1 - proposition 7
      is deliberately uncommented and stands alone.
   6.53 THE METHOD: say nothing except what can be said, and whenever
      someone attempts a metaphysical statement, demonstrate that he
      has given no meaning to certain signs.
        |
        v
TWO READINGS - state both, then rule
   STANDARD / INEFFABILITY .. logical form, value and the mystical
     MANIFEST themselves although they cannot be stated as facts
   RESOLUTE / THERAPEUTIC ... (Diamond, Conant) 6.54 licenses NO body of
     ineffable truths; the apparent doctrines are ELUCIDATORY NONSENSE
   BOTH AGREE: ethics, aesthetics and the mystical are NOT factual
     propositions within logical space.
        |
        v
VERDICT: justified as a DERIVED DIAGNOSIS - one structural error
   generates the whole family of philosophical problems; NOT fully
   justified as a STATEABLE DOCTRINE, because the boundary cannot be
   stated without transgressing itself. 4.112: philosophy is an
   ACTIVITY, not a body of doctrine.
NO VERIFICATION PRINCIPLE ANYWHERE IN THE TRACTATUS - TOPIC 07.

COMPARE BY AXIS, NOT BY PARAGRAPH

THE TWO ATOMISMS
   ATOMS ......... acquainted particulars + qualities/relations (R) vs
                   UNSPECIFIED objects fixed by combinatorial
                   possibility (W)
   GROUND ........ logic + a changing acquaintance epistemology (R) vs
                   a CONDITION-OF-SENSE argument (W)
   INDEPENDENCE .. acknowledged loosely (R) vs STRICTLY insisted upon
                   (W), 2.061 and 4.211
   NEGATION ...... NEGATIVE FACTS accepted (R) vs the TOTALITY account,
                   2.05 and 4.0312 (W)
   EXPRESSION .... 1918 lectures, preface crediting W (R) vs Tractatus,
                   written 1918, German 1921, bilingual 1922 (W)
   RULING: TWO ATOMISMS WITH A COMMON ANCESTRY, NOT ONE DOCTRINE.
        |
        v
CRITICISM LEDGER - always finish with the RESIDUAL
   MOORE     begs the question -> comparative + dialectical replies ->
             RESIDUAL: no NEUTRAL MEASURE of certainty
             paradox of analysis -> knowledge does not wait on analysis
             -> RESIDUAL: the paradox has no settled Moorean solution
   RUSSELL   Strawson 1950 -> truth-conditions are what a logic requires
             -> RESIDUAL: a GENUINE RIVAL ANALYSIS
             logically proper names -> restriction accepted openly ->
             RESIDUAL: the class shrinks almost to nothing
             negative facts -> a truth-making cost -> RESIDUAL: NO
             FIXED FINAL INVENTORY
   TRACTATUS ethics not factual -> deliberate demarcation -> RESIDUAL:
             standard vs resolute disagree on ineffable truths
             self-application -> 6.54 concedes the ladder -> RESIDUAL:
             successful therapy OR unstable doctrine, disputed
             no exemplified simples -> required for determinate sense ->
             RESIDUAL: necessity does not identify an actual base
   THREE DIFFERENT FAILURES: Moore's gap is EPISTEMOLOGICAL, Russell's
   is ONTOLOGICAL, Wittgenstein's concerns the STATUS OF PHILOSOPHICAL
   DISCOURSE. Merging them loses the assessment mark.
        |

PYQ ROUTING - 14 OWNED PARTS OF 112, JOINT-HIGHEST, ONE IN EVERY YEAR
   MOORE (5)      2019 Q3(c) 15 | 2020 Q2(b) 15 | 2021 Q3(a) 20 |
                  2024 Q1(c) 10 | 2025 Q3(c) 15
   RUSSELL (6)    2018 Q4(c) 15 | 2020 Q2(c) 15 | 2022 Q1(b) 10 |

ANSWER SPINE
   10 problem -> analysis -> result -> point
   15 frame -> doctrine -> quote -> key move -> result -> assess
   20 frame -> incomplete symbols -> constructions -> atomism ->
      assess -> close
   DIRECTIVES: descriptions demand NOTATION | quotations demand that you
   LOCATE THE SENTENCE | saying/showing demands the DERIVATION | Moore
   demands the STRUCTURE OF THE REVERSAL | 2020 Q2(c) demands the
   QUOTED PHRASES DECODED | "how is X related to Y" demands SUPPORT,
   NOT ENTAILMENT | comparison demands an AXIS
        |
        v
QUALIFIED CONCLUSION
   Three programmes, one method used for three incompatible purposes,
   each leaving something deliberately unfinished. MOORE redistributes
   the burden of proof and leaves the ANALYSIS open. RUSSELL secures
   ontological economy and leaves the INVENTORY open. EARLY
   WITTGENSTEIN fixes the conditions of sense with unmatched rigour and
   leaves THE STATUS OF HIS OWN BOOK open. Permanent achievements:
   the reversal of the sceptical burden | the quantificational analysis
   of definite descriptions | sense as determinate exclusion within a
   field of possibilities. Permanent costs: no neutral measure of
   certainty | no completed base | no consistent way of stating the
   boundary the Tractatus draws. State an achievement AND its matching
   cost in the same sentence, and the clause has been understood.
